s_{t+1}\sim P^{\pi_{\psi}}(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\left(\widetilde{Q}^{\pi_{\phi}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\pi_{\psi}}(x)}(s_{t},a_{t})-\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\left(\widetilde{Q}^{\pi_{\phi}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\pi_{\psi}}(x)}(s_{t},a_{t})-\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\bigg(\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\bigg(\underset{b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})}{\mathbb{E}}\left[Q^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\bigg)\bigg)\Bigg],

(54)

where the second equality is to expand
b_{t}
from
P^{\pi_{\psi}}
, and the last equality follows from the definition of
\widetilde{Q}^{\pi_{\phi}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\pi_{\psi}}(x)}(s_{t},a_{t})
.

This implies that

\displaystyle\quad\;\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\bigg(\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\bigg(\underset{b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})}{\mathbb{E}}\left[Q^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\bigg)\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{s}d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)\sum_{a}\pi_{\phi}(a|s)\Bigg(\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a|s)\bigg(\sum_{b}\pi_{\psi}(b|s)\left[Q^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s,a,b)-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b|s)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a|s)\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{s}d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)\sum_{a,b}\pi_{\phi}(a|s)\pi_{\psi}(b|s)\left(\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a|s)\left(Q^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s,a,b)-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b|s)+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a|s)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\left(Q^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\right)\right)\right].

(55)

Similarly, we can prove that

\displaystyle\quad\;\nabla_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(\nabla_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})\left(Q^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\right)\right)\right].

(56)

∎

Appendix C
Proofs in Section
3

C.1
Useful Properties of Single-Policy MDPs

The notation in this subsection
differs
from that in the rest of the paper, as we focus exclusively on the properties of single-policy MDPs in this section.

Let
\mathcal{M}(x)=\{\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},r_{x},\mathcal{P}_{x},\gamma,h\}
be a parameterized
single-policy MDP with entropy regularization. For any policy
\pi
, the
soft state-value function at state
s
is defined as

\displaystyle V^{\pi}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s)\triangleq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t})-\tau\log\pi(a_{t}|s_{t})\right)\bigg|s_{0}=s,\pi,\mathcal{M}(x)\right],

where
\tau>0
is the regularization coefficient. Then for a full-support initial state distribution
\rho
, we can define the soft value function of policy
\pi
as
V^{\pi}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\rho}[V^{\pi}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s)]
.
These are all standard definitions and notations in single-policy RL.

For any given
x
, let
V^{*}(x)
be the soft optimal state-value such that
V^{*}_{s}(x)=\max_{\pi}V^{\pi}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s)
. By
30
and
48
,
V^{*}(x)
is the unique fixed point of the softmax Bellman operator
\mathcal{T}_{x}:\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}
defined as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{T}_{x}V)(s)=\tau\log\sum_{a}\exp\left(\frac{r_{x}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V(s^{\prime})}{\tau}\right),

which is
\gamma
-contraction in
V
w.r.t.
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
.
Define the optimal soft Q-value as

\displaystyle Q^{*}_{sa}(x)\triangleq r_{x}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x),

then the optimal policy
\pi^{*}_{x}
is given by

\displaystyle\pi^{*}_{x}(a|s)=\frac{\exp(Q^{*}_{sa}(x)/\tau)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}\exp(Q^{*}_{sa^{\prime}}(x)/\tau)}.

Now we make some standard assumptions on the parameterized MDP
\mathcal{M}(x)
.

Assumption C.1
.

Suppose the following conditions hold for
\mathcal{M}(x)
:

1.

Bounded reward:
|r_{x}(s,a)|\leq R_{\max}
for any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
and any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
.

2.

Lipschitz reward:
\sup_{s,a}|r_{x_{1}}(s,a)-r_{x_{2}}(s,a)|\leq C_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|
for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
.

3.

Lipschitz transition:
\sup_{s,a}\|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{1}\leq C_{P}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|
for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
.

Then we can establish the following Lipschitz continuity of the optimal soft state-value and soft Q-value w.r.t.
x
.

Lemma C.1
.

Under Assumption
C.1
, the optimal soft state-value
V^{*}(x)
is
C_{V}
-Lipschitz continuous in
x
for some constant
C_{V}>0
. The optimal soft Q-value
Q^{*}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
is
C_{Q}
-Lipschitz continuous in
x
for some constant
C_{Q}>0
.

Proof.

For any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
and any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)^{T}V-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)^{T}V|\leq\|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{1}\|V\|_{\infty}\leq C_{P}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\|V\|_{\infty}.

Thus, we can obtain

\displaystyle\quad\,\sup_{s,a}\left|\left(r_{x_{1}}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V_{s^{\prime}}\right)-\left(r_{x_{2}}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V_{s^{\prime}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq(C_{r}+\gamma C_{P}\|V\|_{\infty})\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|.

(57)

By the
1
-Lipschitz continuity of the log-sum-exp function in
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
, we have for any
v,w\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}|}
,

\displaystyle|\tau\log\sum_{a}e^{v_{a}/\tau}-\tau\log\sum_{a}e^{w_{a}/\tau}|\leq\|v-w\|_{\infty}=\sup_{a}|v_{a}-w_{a}|,

and hence

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{x_{1}}V-\mathcal{T}_{x_{2}}V\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\sup_{s}|(\mathcal{T}_{x_{1}}V)(s)-(\mathcal{T}_{x_{2}}V)(s)|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,a}\left|\left(r_{x_{1}}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V_{s^{\prime}}\right)-\left(r_{x_{2}}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V_{s^{\prime}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq(C_{r}+\gamma C_{P}\|V\|_{\infty})\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|.

(58)

Since for any
x
,
V^{*}(x)
is the unique fixed point of
\mathcal{T}_{x}

(
30
)
, i.e.,
\mathcal{T}_{x}V^{*}(x)=V^{*}(x)
, then

\displaystyle\|V^{*}(x_{1})-V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{T}_{x_{1}}V^{*}(x_{1})-\mathcal{T}_{x_{2}}V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{T}_{x_{1}}V^{*}(x_{1})-\mathcal{T}_{x_{1}}V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}+\|\mathcal{T}_{x_{1}}V^{*}(x_{2})-\mathcal{T}_{x_{2}}V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\|V^{*}(x_{1})-V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}+(C_{r}+\gamma C_{P}\|V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty})\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,

which implies

\displaystyle\|V^{*}(x_{1})-V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{r}+\gamma C_{P}\|V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}}{1-\gamma}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|.

(59)

Since for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
and any
s\in\mathcal{S}
,

\displaystyle\left|(\mathcal{T}_{x}\mathbf{0})(s)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\tau\log\sum_{a}\exp\left(\frac{r_{x}(s,a)}{\tau}\right)\right|\leq R_{\max}+\tau\log|\mathcal{A}|,

then by
\gamma
-contraction of
\mathcal{T}_{x}
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
, we can obtain,

\displaystyle\|V^{*}(x)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{T}_{x}V^{*}(x)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{T}_{x}V^{*}(x)-\mathcal{T}_{x}\mathbf{0}\|_{\infty}+\|\mathcal{T}_{x}\mathbf{0}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\|V^{*}(x)\|_{\infty}+R_{\max}+\tau\log|\mathcal{A}|,

which implies
\|V^{*}(x)\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{R_{\max}+\tau\log|\mathcal{A}|}{1-\gamma}
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
. We use
B_{V}
here to denote
\frac{R_{\max}+\tau\log|\mathcal{A}|}{1-\gamma}
for brevity.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|V^{*}(x_{1})-V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{r}+\gamma C_{P}B_{V}}{1-\gamma}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,

(60)

which completes the proof for the Lipschitz continuity of
V^{*}(x)
by applying
\|\cdot\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}|}\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
.

Finally, we prove the Lipschitz continuity of
Q^{*}(x)
. For any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,|Q^{*}_{sa}(x_{1})-Q^{*}_{sa}(x_{2})|

\displaystyle=|r_{x_{1}}(s,a)-r_{x_{2}}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{1})-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{2})\right)|

\displaystyle\leq|r_{x_{1}}(s,a)-r_{x_{2}}(s,a)|+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{1})-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)(V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{1})-V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{2}))|+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}|(\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a))V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\gamma\|V^{*}(x_{1})-V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}+\gamma\|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{1}\|V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\gamma\frac{C_{r}+\gamma C_{P}B_{V}}{1-\gamma}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\gamma C_{P}B_{V}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,

(61)

which completes the proof by applying
\|Q^{*}(x_{1})-Q^{*}(x_{2})\|\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}\max_{s,a}|Q^{*}_{sa}(x_{1})-Q^{*}_{sa}(x_{2})|
.

∎

Lemma C.2
.

Under Assumption
C.1
, let

\pi^{*}(x)=\arg\max_{\pi}V^{\pi}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
be the optimal soft policy for
the MDP
\mathcal{M}(x)
. Then there exists a constant
C_{\pi^{*}}>0
such that

\pi^{*}(x)
is
C_{\pi^{*}}
-Lipschitz continuous in
x
.

Proof.

By
30
, the optimal soft policy for any
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
can be represented as

\displaystyle\pi^{*}_{sa}(x)=\frac{\exp(Q^{*}_{sa}(x)/\tau)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}\exp(Q^{*}_{sa^{\prime}}(x)/\tau)},

(62)

where
Q^{*}(x)
is the optimal soft Q-value defined in Lemma
C.1
.

Therefore, for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\pi^{*}(x_{1})-\pi^{*}(x_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\tau}\|Q^{*}(x_{1})-Q^{*}(x_{2})\|\leq\frac{C_{Q}}{2\tau}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,

(63)

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.3
.

Under Assumption
C.1
, suppose the reward model
r_{x}
and the transition model
\mathcal{P}_{x}
also satisfy the following conditions:

1.

r_{x}(s,a)
is differentiable w.r.t.
x
and there exists a constant
L_{r}
such that for any
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
, we have
\sup_{s,a}\|\nabla r_{x_{1}}(s,a)-\nabla r_{x_{2}}(s,a)\|\leq L_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|
.

2.

\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)
is differentiable w.r.t.
x
and there exists a constant
L_{P}
such that for any
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
, we have
\sup_{s,a,s^{\prime}}\|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)-\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\|\leq L_{P}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|
.

Let
\pi^{*}(x)=\arg\max_{\pi}V^{\pi}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
, then there exists a constant
L_{\pi}
such that for any
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\pi^{*}(x_{1})-\nabla\pi^{*}(x_{2})\|\leq L_{\pi}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|.

Proof.

For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
a\in\mathcal{A}
,

\displaystyle\pi^{*}_{sa}(x)=\frac{\exp(Q^{*}_{sa}(x)/\tau)}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}\exp(Q^{*}_{sa^{\prime}}(x)/\tau)}=\exp\left(\frac{Q^{*}_{sa}(x)}{\tau}-\log\sum_{a^{\prime}}\exp\left(\frac{Q^{*}_{sa^{\prime}}(x)}{\tau}\right)\right).

By the definition of
Q^{*}_{sa}(x)
, we have

\displaystyle Q^{*}_{sa}(x)=r_{x}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x).

This implies that

\displaystyle\log\sum_{a^{\prime}}\exp\left(\frac{Q^{*}_{sa^{\prime}}(x)}{\tau}\right)

\displaystyle=\log\sum_{a^{\prime}}\exp\left(\frac{r_{x}(s,a^{\prime})+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a^{\prime})V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x)}{\tau}\right)

\displaystyle=\tau^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{x}V^{*}(x))(s)=\tau^{-1}V^{*}_{s}(x).

Thus, we can represent
\pi^{*}_{sa}(x)
as

\displaystyle\pi^{*}_{sa}(x)=\exp\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\left(Q^{*}_{sa}(x)-V^{*}_{s}(x)\right)\right).

(64)

Taking the gradient with respect to
x
on both sides, we can obtain

\displaystyle\nabla\pi^{*}_{sa}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{*}_{sa}(x)}{\tau}\left(\nabla r_{x}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\nabla V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x)-\nabla V^{*}_{s}(x)\right).

In matrix form, this can be written as

\displaystyle\nabla\pi^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau}\diag(\pi^{*}(x))\left(\nabla r_{x}+\gamma D_{P}(x)-U(x)\nabla V^{*}(x)\right),

(65)

where each element
U\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|\times|\mathcal{S}|}
is defined as
U_{sas^{\prime}}\triangleq 1-\gamma\mathcal{P}_{sas^{\prime}}(x)
for
s=s^{\prime}
and
U_{sas^{\prime}}\triangleq-\gamma\mathcal{P}_{sas^{\prime}}(x)
otherwise, and each element of
D_{\mathcal{P}}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|\times d_{x}}
is defined as

\displaystyle D_{\mathcal{P}}(x)_{sa}\triangleq\sum_{s^{\prime}}V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x)\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)^{T}.

Consider the matrix
U(x)
, we have

\displaystyle\|U(x)\|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s,a,s^{\prime}\\
s\neq s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\gamma^{2}\mathcal{P}_{sas^{\prime}}(x)^{2}+\sum_{s,a}(1-\gamma\mathcal{P}_{sas}(x))^{2}}\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}\triangleq B_{U},

(66)

and

\displaystyle\|U(x_{1})-U(x_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}\sup_{s,a}\sum_{s^{\prime}}|U_{sas^{\prime}}(x_{1})-U_{sas^{\prime}}(x_{2})|\leq\gamma C_{P}\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\triangleq C_{U}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|.

(67)

Consider the transition gradient term
D_{\mathcal{P}}(x)
, we have for any state
s
and action
a
,

\displaystyle\|D_{\mathcal{P}}(x)_{sa}\|

\displaystyle=\|\sum_{s^{\prime}}V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x)\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)^{T}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s^{\prime}}|V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x)|\|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{S}|B_{V}C_{P}.

Thus, we can obtain

\displaystyle\|D_{\mathcal{P}}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}\sup_{s,a}\|D_{\mathcal{P}}(x)_{sa}\|\leq|\mathcal{S}|^{\frac{3}{2}}|\mathcal{A}|^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{V}C_{P}\triangleq B_{D_{\mathcal{P}}},

(68)

and for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
, any state
s
and action
a
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\;\left\|D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{1})_{sa}-D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{2})_{sa}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{s^{\prime}}V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{1})\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)^{T}-\sum_{s^{\prime}}V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{2})\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)^{T}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s^{\prime}}\left\|V_{s^{\prime}}^{*}(x_{1})\left(\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)^{T}-\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)^{T}\right)\right\|+\sum_{s^{\prime}}\left\|\left(V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{1})-V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{2})\right)\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)^{T}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{S}|B_{V}\sup_{s^{\prime}}\|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)-\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\|+|\mathcal{S}|C_{P}\|V^{*}(x_{1})-V^{*}(x_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left(|\mathcal{S}|B_{V}L_{P}+|\mathcal{S}|C_{P}\frac{C_{r}+\gamma C_{P}B_{V}}{1-\gamma}\right)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle\triangleq C_{D_{\mathcal{P}}}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,

(69)

where the second last inequality is due to the Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x}
and
V^{*}(x)
w.r.t.
x
.

Moreover, define the mapping
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\times\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}\mapsto\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}
as

\displaystyle\Phi(x,v)=\tau\log\left(\exp\left(\frac{r(x)+\gamma\mathcal{P}(x)v}{\tau}\right)\cdot\mathbf{1}\right),

where
\log(\cdot)
and
\exp(\cdot)
are element-wise operations, and
\mathbf{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{A}|}
is an all-one vector. Then
V^{*}(x)
is the fixed point of this mapping.

By Proposition 4.1 in
48
, we know that
V^{*}(x)
is the unique fixed point of the mapping
\Phi(x,\cdot)
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
, and
V^{*}(x)
is differentiable w.r.t.
x
with

\displaystyle\nabla V^{*}(x)

\displaystyle=(I-\gamma\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x)}_{x})^{-1}\nabla_{1}\Phi(x,V^{*}(x))

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x)}_{x}\right)^{t}\nabla_{1}\Phi(x,V^{*}(x)),

(70)

where
\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\pi^{*}(x)}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{S}|}
is the transition kernel induced by the optimal policy
\pi^{*}(x)
, with each element defined as
\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x)}_{x}\right)_{ss^{\prime}}=\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x)\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)
.

Consequently, for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla V^{*}(x_{1})-\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}\right)^{t}\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{1},V^{*}(x_{1}))-\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\right)^{t}\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{2},V^{*}(x_{2}))\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}\right)^{t}\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{1},V^{*}(x_{1}))-\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\right)^{t}\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{1},V^{*}(x_{1}))\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\right)^{t}\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{1},V^{*}(x_{1}))-\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\right)^{t}\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{2},V^{*}(x_{2}))\right)\right\|.

Moreover, we have for any state
s
,

\displaystyle\quad\,\|\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s)-\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\sum_{a}\pi^{*}(a|s;x_{1})\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)-\sum_{a}\pi^{*}(a|s;x_{2})\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\sum_{a}\left(\pi^{*}(a|s;x_{1})-\pi^{*}(a|s;x_{2})\right)\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{\infty}+\|\sum_{a}\pi^{*}(a|s;x_{1})\left(\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)\right)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a}|\pi^{*}(a|s;x_{1})-\pi^{*}(a|s;x_{2})|\|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{\infty}+\sum_{a}\pi^{*}(a|s;x_{1})\|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\pi^{*}(\cdot|s;x_{1})-\pi^{*}(\cdot|s;x_{2})\|_{1}+\sup_{s,a}\|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}|}\|\pi^{*}(x_{1})-\pi^{*}(x_{2})\|+\sup_{s,a}\|\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s,a)-\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s,a)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}|}C_{\pi^{*}}+C_{P}\right)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,

(71)

therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}-\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\|_{\infty}\leq|\mathcal{S}|\sup_{s}\|\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}(\cdot|s)-\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}(\cdot|s)\|_{\infty}\leq|\mathcal{S}|\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}|}C_{\pi^{*}}+C_{P}\right)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|.

Hence, denote
A=\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{S}|}
,
B=\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{S}|}
. We obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}\right)^{t}-\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\right)^{t}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|A^{t}-B^{t}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}A^{t-1-i}(A-B)B^{i}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\|A^{t-1-i}\|_{\infty}\|A-B\|_{\infty}\|B^{i}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq t\|A-B\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq t|\mathcal{S}|\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}|}C_{\pi^{*}}+C_{P}\right)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,

(72)

which implies that there exists a constant
L_{pow}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}\right)^{t}-\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\right)^{t}\right\|\leq|\mathcal{S}|\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}\right)^{t}-\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\right)^{t}\right\|_{\infty}\leq tL_{pow}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|.

(73)

Meanwhile, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Phi_{s}(x,v)}{\partial x_{i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{a}\left(\partial_{x_{i}}r_{x}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\partial_{x_{i}}\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)v_{s^{\prime}}\right)\exp\left(\tau^{-1}\left(r_{x}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)v_{s^{\prime}}\right)\right)}{\sum_{a}\exp\left(\tau^{-1}\left(r_{x}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)v_{s^{\prime}}\right)\right)},

and substituting
v=V^{*}(x)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{1}\Phi_{s}(x,V^{*}(x))

\displaystyle=\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x)\left(\nabla r_{x}(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x)\right).

(74)

Then for any
x
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{1}\Phi_{s}(x,V^{*}(x))\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x)\left(\|\nabla r_{x}(s,a)\|+\gamma\sum_{s^{\prime}}\|\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\||V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x)|\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{r}+\gamma|\mathcal{S}|C_{P}B_{V}\triangleq B_{\Phi},

(75)

thus, we can arrive at

\displaystyle\|\nabla V^{*}(x)\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x)}_{x}\right)^{t}\nabla_{1}\Phi(x,V^{*}(x))\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x)}_{x}\right)^{t}\|\|\nabla_{1}\Phi(x,V^{*}(x))\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}|}B_{\Phi}}{1-\gamma}.

(76)

We also have for any state
s
and for any
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\|\nabla_{1}\Phi_{s}(x_{1},V^{*}(x_{1}))-\nabla_{1}\Phi_{s}(x_{2},V^{*}(x_{2}))\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{1})\nabla r_{x_{1}}(s,a)-\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{2})\nabla r_{x_{2}}(s,a)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\;+\gamma\left\|\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{1})\sum_{s^{\prime}}\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{1})-\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}}\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sum_{a}\left(\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{1})-\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{2})\right)\nabla r_{x_{1}}(s,a)\right\|+\left\|\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{2})\left(\nabla r_{x_{1}}(s,a)-\nabla r_{x_{2}}(s,a)\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\;+\gamma\left\|\sum_{a}\left(\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{1})-\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{2})\right)\sum_{s^{\prime}}\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{1})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\;+\gamma\left\|\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}}\left(\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)-\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\right)V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{1})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\;+\gamma\left\|\sum_{a}\pi^{*}_{sa}(x_{2})\sum_{s^{\prime}}\nabla\mathcal{P}_{x_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\left(V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{1})-V^{*}_{s^{\prime}}(x_{2})\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{r}\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}|}C_{\pi^{*}}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+L_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\gamma|\mathcal{S}|C_{P}B_{V}\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}|}C_{\pi^{*}}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle\quad\;+\gamma|\mathcal{S}|L_{P}B_{V}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\gamma|\mathcal{S}|C_{P}C_{V}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}|}C_{r}C_{\pi^{*}}+L_{r}+\gamma|\mathcal{S}|C_{P}B_{V}\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}|}C_{\pi^{*}}+\gamma|\mathcal{S}|L_{P}B_{V}+\gamma|\mathcal{S}|C_{P}C_{V}\right)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,

(77)

which implies that there exists a constant
L_{\Phi}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{1},V^{*}(x_{1}))-\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{2},V^{*}(x_{2}))\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}|}\max_{s}\|\nabla_{1}\Phi_{s}(x_{1},V^{*}(x_{1}))-\nabla_{1}\Phi_{s}(x_{2},V^{*}(x_{2}))\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{\Phi}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|.

(78)

Subsequently, we can arrive at for any
x_{1},x_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla V^{*}(x_{1})-\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{1})}_{x_{1}}\right)^{t}-\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\right)^{t}\right\|\left\|\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{1},V^{*}(x_{1}))\right\|

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left\|\left(\mathcal{P}^{\pi^{*}(x_{2})}_{x_{2}}\right)^{t}\right\|\left\|\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{1},V^{*}(x_{1}))-\nabla_{1}\Phi(x_{2},V^{*}(x_{2}))\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}tL_{pow}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|B_{\Phi}+\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}|}L_{\Phi}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\gamma L_{pow}B_{\Phi}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}|}L_{\Phi}}{1-\gamma}\right)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|\triangleq L_{V}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|.

(79)

Therefore, we have for any
x_{1},x_{2}
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\|\nabla\pi^{*}(x_{1})-\nabla\pi^{*}(x_{2})\|

\displaystyle=\big\|\tau^{-1}\operatorname{diag}(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))\left(\nabla r(x_{1})+\gamma D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{1})-U(x_{1})\nabla V^{*}(x_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\;-\tau^{-1}\operatorname{diag}(\pi^{*}(x_{2}))\left(\nabla r(x_{2})+\gamma D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{2})-U(x_{2})\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\right)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{-1}\left\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))\nabla r(x_{1})-\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{2}))\nabla r(x_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\gamma\left\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{1})-\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{2}))D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\left\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))U(x_{1})\nabla V^{*}(x_{1})-\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{2}))U(x_{2})\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{-1}\left\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))\left(\nabla r(x_{1})-\nabla r(x_{2})\right)\right\|+\tau^{-1}\left\|\left(\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))-\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{2}))\right)\nabla r(x_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\gamma\left\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))\left(D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{1})-D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{2})\right)\right\|+\tau^{-1}\gamma\left\|\left(\diag\left(\pi^{*}(x_{1})\right)-\diag\left(\pi^{*}(x_{2})\right)\right)D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\left\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))U(x_{1})\left(\nabla V^{*}(x_{1})-\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\left\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))\left(U(x_{1})-U(x_{2})\right)\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\left\|\left(\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))-\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{2}))\right)U(x_{2})\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{-1}\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))\|\|\nabla r(x_{1})-\nabla r(x_{2})\|+\tau^{-1}\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))-\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{2}))\|\|\nabla r(x_{2})\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\gamma\left\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))\right\|\left\|D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{1})-D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{2})\right\|+\tau^{-1}\gamma\left\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))-\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{2}))\right\|\left\|D_{\mathcal{P}}(x_{2})\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))\|\|U(x_{1})\|\|\nabla V^{*}(x_{1})-\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))\|\|U(x_{1})-U(x_{2})\|\|\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\|\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{1}))-\diag(\pi^{*}(x_{2}))\|\|U(x_{2})\|\|\nabla V^{*}(x_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{-1}\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}L_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\tau^{-1}\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}C_{\pi^{*}}C_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}\gamma C_{D_{\mathcal{P}}}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\tau^{-1}\gamma C_{\pi^{*}}B_{D_{\mathcal{P}}}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau^{-1}B_{U}L_{V}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\tau^{-1}\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}|}C_{U}B_{\Phi}}{1-\gamma}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\tau^{-1}\frac{B_{U}B_{\Phi}C_{\pi^{*}}}{1-\gamma}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\tau^{-1}\left(\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}\left(L_{r}+C_{\pi^{*}}C_{r}\right)+\gamma C_{D_{\mathcal{P}}}+\gamma C_{\pi^{*}}B_{D_{\mathcal{P}}}+B_{U}L_{V}+\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{S}|}C_{U}B_{\Phi}}{1-\gamma}+\frac{B_{U}B_{\Phi}C_{\pi^{*}}}{1-\gamma}\right)\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

(80)

\displaystyle\triangleq L_{\pi}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|,

(81)

where the second inequality is to separate the differences of each term, and the third inequality follows from the sub-multiplicative property of matrix norms.
∎

C.2
Useful Properties of Min–Max Zero-Sum Markov Games

For the regularized MMZSMG
\mathcal{M}(x)=\{\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},r_{x},\mathcal{P},\gamma\}
defined in Section
2
,
we define the state-visitation distribution under policies
\pi_{\phi}
and
\pi_{\psi}
as

\displaystyle d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)\triangleq(1-\gamma)\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}P(s_{t}=s|s_{0}\sim\rho,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi},\mathcal{M}(x)).

Consider the following two best-response optimization problems:
\max_{\pi_{\psi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{B}|}}V_{\mathcal{M}(x)}^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}(\rho)
and
\min_{\pi_{\phi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{A}|}}V_{\mathcal{M}(x)}^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}(\rho)
. We have the following lemma.

Lemma C.4
.

For any
x
and any fixed

\pi_{\phi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{A}|}
, the problem

\max_{\pi_{\psi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{B}|}}V_{\mathcal{M}(x)}^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}(\rho)
admits a unique maximizer. Similarly, for any fixed

\pi_{\psi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{B}|}
, the problem
\min_{\pi_{\phi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{A}|}}V_{\mathcal{M}(x)}^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}(\rho)
admits a unique minimizer.

Proof.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)

\displaystyle=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left[r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})\right]\bigg]

\displaystyle=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P^{\pi_{\psi}}(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left[r^{\pi_{\psi}}_{x}(s_{t},a_{t})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})+\tau_{\psi}H(\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t}))\right]\bigg],

(82)

where
P^{\pi_{\psi}}(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})}[P(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})]
and
r^{\pi_{\psi}}_{x}(s_{t},a_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})}[r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})]
.

Here
H(\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t}))
does not depend on
\pi_{\phi}
. Since
\pi_{\phi}
is the min-player, we can define a new reward model for
\pi_{\phi}
as:

\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}(s,a)\triangleq-r^{\pi_{\psi}}_{x}(s,a)-\tau_{\psi}H(\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s)).

Then for any given
x
and
\psi
,
\mathcal{M}(x)
is equivalent to a single-policy entropy-regularized MDP as
\mathcal{M}^{\pi_{\psi}}(x)=\{\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P^{\pi_{\psi}},\tilde{r}_{(x,\psi)},\gamma\}
, and
\pi_{\phi}
is the policy that wants to maximize the soft value function:

\displaystyle J^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi)=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P^{\pi_{\psi}}(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left[\tilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}(s_{t},a_{t})-\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\right]\bigg],

(83)

which is equivalent to
-V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
.

Since we know that in the single-policy setting, the optimal policy for the soft value function exists and is unique
(
13
)
, then for any given
x
and
\psi
, the optimal
\pi_{\phi}
that maximizes the soft value function
J^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi)
also exists and is unique. Therefore, the minimizer of the problem

\min_{\pi_{\phi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{A}|}}V_{\mathcal{M}(x)}^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}(\rho)

for any fixed
x
and
\pi_{\psi}
also exists and is unique.

Similarly,
V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
can also be written as

\displaystyle V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)

\displaystyle=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P^{\pi_{\phi}}(\cdot|s_{t},b_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left[r^{\pi_{\phi}}_{x}(s_{t},b_{t})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})-\tau_{\phi}H(\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t}))\right]\bigg],

(84)

where
P^{\pi_{\phi}}(\cdot|s_{t},b_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})}[P(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})]
and
r^{\pi_{\phi}}_{x}(s_{t},b_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})}[r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})]
.

Define

\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{(x,\phi)}(s,b)\triangleq r^{\pi_{\phi}}_{x}(s,b)-\tau_{\phi}H(\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s)).

Then, for any fixed
x
and
\pi_{\phi}
, by absorbing the effect of

\pi_{\phi}
into the transition kernel and the reward, the optimization over

\pi_{\psi}
can be reformulated as a single-policy entropy-regularized MDP
\mathcal{M}^{\pi_{\phi}}(x)=\{\mathcal{S},\mathcal{B},P^{\pi_{\phi}},\tilde{r}_{(x,\phi)},\gamma\}
. The corresponding objective for
\pi_{\psi}
is to maximize the following soft value function:

\displaystyle J^{\pi_{\phi}}(x,\psi)=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P^{\pi_{\phi}}(\cdot|s_{t},b_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left[\tilde{r}_{(x,\phi)}(s_{t},b_{t})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})\right]\bigg],

(85)

which is equivalent to
V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
.
Then for any given
x
and
\phi
, the optimal
\pi_{\psi}
that maximizes
J^{\pi_{\phi}}(x,\psi)
exists and is unique. Therefore, the maximizer of the problem
\max_{\pi_{\psi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{B}|}}V_{\mathcal{M}(x)}^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}(\rho)
for any
x
and
\phi
also exists and is unique.
∎

Denote the best-response policies as
\pi^{*}_{\phi}(x,\psi)=\arg\min_{\pi_{\phi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{A}|}}V_{\mathcal{M}(x)}^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}(\rho)
,
\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi)=\arg\max_{\pi_{\psi}\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{S}|}^{|\mathcal{B}|}}V_{\mathcal{M}(x)}^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}(\rho)
. Denote the corresponding best-response parameters as

\phi^{*}(x,\psi)=\arg\min_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)
,
and
\psi^{*}(x,\phi)=\arg\max_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)
.
Then the optimal softmax parameters satisfy

\displaystyle\phi^{*}(x,\psi)=\{\phi\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}:\pi_{\phi}=\pi^{*}_{\phi}(x,\psi)\}=\{\log\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi)+\diag(c)\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{A}|}:c\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}\},

and

\displaystyle\psi^{*}(x,\phi)=\{\psi\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{B}|}:\pi_{\psi}=\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi)\}=\{\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)+\diag(c)\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{B}|}:c\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}\},

where
\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{A}|}
is a
|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{A}|
matrix with all entries being
1
.

Similarly, we also define the best-response state-visitation distribution for policy
\pi_{\phi}
as

d^{\pi_{\phi},{\dagger}}_{\rho}(s)\triangleq d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)\big|_{\pi_{\psi}\in\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi)}
,
and the best-response state-visitation distribution for policy
\pi_{\psi}
as

d^{{\dagger},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)\triangleq d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)\big|_{\pi_{\phi}\in\pi^{*}_{\phi}(x,\psi)}
.

By Proposition
1
, we know that the optimal policy pair
(\pi_{\phi}^{*},\pi_{\psi}^{*})
exists and is unique, therefore we can also define the optimal state-visitation distribution under policies
\pi_{\phi}^{*}
and
\pi_{\psi}^{*}
as

d^{*}_{\rho}(s)\triangleq d^{\pi_{\phi}^{*},\pi_{\psi}^{*}}_{\rho}(s)
.

Denote
J^{*}(x)=\min_{\phi}\max_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)=V^{\star}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
. Then we have the following lemma on the PŁ condition of the best-response value function
J_{1}(x,\phi)=\max_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)
and
J_{2}(x,\psi)=\min_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)
.

Lemma C.5

(Non-uniform PŁ condition on best-response value function)
.

Suppose the initial distribution
\rho
has full support, i.e.,
\min_{s}\rho(s)>0
.
Then for any
x
, the best-response value function
J_{1}(x,\phi)=\max_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)
satisfies the following condition in
\phi
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J_{1}(x,\phi)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu_{1}(x,\phi)\left(J_{1}(x,\phi)-J^{*}(x)\right),

where

\displaystyle\mu_{1}(x,\phi)=\frac{\tau_{\phi}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho(s)\min_{s,a}\pi_{\phi}^{2}(a|s)\min_{s}\frac{d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi)}_{\rho}(s)}{d^{{\dagger},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi)}_{\rho}(s)},

and
\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi)=\arg\max_{\pi_{\psi}}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho).

Similarly, for any
x
, the best-response value function
J_{2}(x,\psi)=\min_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)
satisfies the following condition in
\psi
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\psi}J_{2}(x,\psi)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu_{2}(x,\psi)\left(J^{*}(x)-J_{2}(x,\psi)\right),

where

\displaystyle\mu_{2}(x,\psi)=\frac{\tau_{\psi}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho(s)\min_{s,b}\pi_{\psi}^{2}(b|s)\min_{s}\frac{d^{\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi),\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)}{d^{\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi),{\dagger}}_{\rho}(s)},

and
\pi^{*}_{\phi}(x,\psi)=\arg\min_{\pi_{\phi}}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho).

Proof.

Denote
V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi})=V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
for notational simplicity if there is no confusion.

Define a new reward model for
\pi_{\phi}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}(s,a)=-r^{\pi_{\psi}}_{x}(s,a)-\tau_{\psi}H(\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s)),

and the transition probability as
P^{\pi_{\psi}}(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t})=\mathbb{E}_{b_{t}\sim\pi_{\psi}(\cdot|s_{t})}[P(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})]
.

Then, for any fixed
x
and
\pi_{\psi}
, the original minimization over

\pi_{\phi}
can be equivalently reformulated as a maximization problem in the
single-policy entropy-regularized MDP

\mathcal{M}^{\pi_{\psi}}(x)=\{\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},P^{\pi_{\psi}},\tilde{r}_{(x,\psi)},\gamma\}.

Under the transformed reward
\tilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}
, the corresponding
objective for
\pi_{\phi}
is to maximize

\displaystyle J^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi)=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\sim\rho,a_{t}\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s_{t})\\
s_{t+1}\sim P^{\pi_{\psi}}(\cdot|s_{t},a_{t})\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\left[\tilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}(s_{t},a_{t})-\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\right]\bigg],

(86)

which equals
-V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
.
Then the state-visitation distribution under policy
\pi_{\phi}
in this MDP is exactly
d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}
.

By Lemma 15 in
28
, we have for any
x
, and any given
\psi
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mu^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi)\left(\max_{\phi}J^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi)-J^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi)\right),

(87)

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mu^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi)\left(V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi})-\min_{\phi}V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi})\right),

(88)

where

\displaystyle\mu^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi):=\frac{\tau_{\phi}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho(s)\min_{s,a}\pi_{\phi}^{2}(a|s)\min_{s}\frac{d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)}{d^{{\dagger},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)}.

Now for an arbitrary
\pi_{\phi}
, we have
\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)=\arg\max_{\pi_{\psi}}V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi})
is unique. Thus, by Lemma
A.2
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{\phi}J_{1}(x,\phi)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\phi}\max_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\phi}\max_{\pi_{\psi}}V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\phi}V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)).

(89)

Then we can get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J_{1}(x,\phi)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi))\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mu^{\pi_{\psi^{*}(x,\pi_{\phi})}}(x,\pi_{\phi})\left(V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi))-\min_{\phi^{\prime}}V(x,\pi_{\phi^{\prime}},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi))\right)

\displaystyle=\mu_{1}(x,\phi)\left(J_{1}(x,\phi)-V(x,\tilde{\pi}_{\phi}^{*},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi))\right),

(90)

where
\tilde{\pi}_{\phi}^{*}=\arg\min_{\pi_{\phi}^{\prime}}V(x,\pi_{\phi}^{\prime},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi))
for the given
\phi
and

\displaystyle\mu_{1}(x,\phi)\triangleq\mu^{\pi_{\psi^{*}(x,\pi_{\phi})}}(x,\pi_{\phi})=\frac{\tau_{\phi}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho(s)\min_{s,a}\pi_{\phi}^{2}(a|s)\min_{s}\frac{d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi)}_{\rho}(s)}{d^{{\dagger},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi)}_{\rho}(s)}.

Consequently, for any given
\phi
, by Proposition
B.1
, we can get

\displaystyle\min_{\phi^{\prime}}J_{1}(x,\phi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\min_{\pi_{\phi}^{\prime}}\max_{\pi_{\psi}}V(x,\pi_{\phi}^{\prime},\pi_{\psi})

\displaystyle=\max_{\pi_{\psi}}\min_{\pi_{\phi}^{\prime}}V(x,\pi_{\phi}^{\prime},\pi_{\psi})

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\pi_{\phi}^{\prime}}V(x,\pi_{\phi}^{\prime},\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi))

\displaystyle=V(x,\tilde{\pi}_{\phi}^{*},\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\pi_{\phi})).

(91)

On the other hand, by definition, we have

\displaystyle\min_{\phi^{\prime}}J_{1}(x,\phi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\min_{\pi_{\phi}^{\prime}}\max_{\pi_{\psi}}V(x,\pi_{\phi}^{\prime},\pi_{\psi})=J^{*}(x).

(92)

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J_{1}(x,\pi_{\phi})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{1}(x,\phi)\left(J_{1}(x,\phi)-V(x,\tilde{\pi}_{\phi}^{*},\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi))\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{1}(x,\phi)\left(J_{1}(x,\phi)-J^{*}(x)\right).

(93)

Similarly, we have for any
x

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\psi}J_{2}(x,\psi)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{2}(x,\psi)\left(J^{*}(x)-J_{2}(x,\psi)\right),

(94)

where

\displaystyle\mu_{2}(x,\psi)=\frac{\tau_{\psi}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho(s)\min_{s,b}\pi_{\psi}^{2}(b|s)\min_{s}\frac{d^{\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi),\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)}{d^{\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi),{\dagger}}_{\rho}(s)}.

and
\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi)=\arg\min_{\pi_{\phi}}V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi})
.

∎

Lemma C.6

(Lemma
1
in the main text)
.

Let
\theta=(\phi,\psi)\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|+|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{B}|}
denote the
concatenation of the policy parameters
\phi
and
\psi
. Suppose the initial
distribution
\rho
has full support, i.e.,
\min_{s}\rho(s)>0
. Then, for any

x
and any
\theta
,
g(x,\theta)
satisfies the following condition:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}g(x,\theta)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu(x,\theta)g(x,\theta),

(95)

where

\displaystyle\mu(x,\theta)=(1-\gamma)\frac{\min\{\tau_{\phi},\tau_{\psi}\}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho^{2}(s)\min\{\min_{s,a}\pi^{2}_{\phi}(a|s),\min_{s,b}\pi^{2}_{\psi}(b|s)\}>0.

Proof.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}g(x,\theta)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\nabla_{\phi}J_{1}(x,\phi)\\
-\nabla_{\psi}J_{2}(x,\psi)\end{bmatrix},

(96)

Hence, by Lemma
C.5
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}g(x,\theta)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J_{1}(x,\phi)\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\psi}J_{2}(x,\psi)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(\mu_{1}(x,\phi)(J_{1}(x,\phi)-J^{*}(x))+\mu_{2}(x,\psi)(J^{*}(x)-J_{2}(x,\psi))\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\min\{\mu_{1}(x,\phi),\mu_{2}(x,\psi)\}\left(J_{1}(x,\phi)-J_{2}(x,\psi)\right)

\displaystyle=\min\{\mu_{1}(x,\phi),\mu_{2}(x,\psi)\}g(x,\theta).

(97)

Since for any
\phi,\psi
, and for any state
s
,

\displaystyle d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\Pr(s_{t}=s|s_{0}\sim\rho,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi},\mathcal{M}(x))

\displaystyle\geq(1-\gamma)\min_{s}\rho(s)>0,

then for any
\phi,\phi^{\prime},\psi,\psi^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\min_{s}\frac{d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)}{d^{\pi_{\phi^{\prime}},\pi_{\psi^{\prime}}}_{\rho}(s)}\geq(1-\gamma)\min_{s}\rho(s)>0.

Therefore, taking

\displaystyle\mu(x,\theta)=(1-\gamma)\frac{\min\{\tau_{\phi},\tau_{\psi}\}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho^{2}(s)\min\{\min_{s,a}\pi^{2}_{\phi}(a|s),\min_{s,b}\pi^{2}_{\psi}(b|s)\},

we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}g(x,\theta)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu(x,\theta)g(x,\theta).

(98)

∎

C.3
Proofs of Other Technical Lemmas

Lemma C.7
.

Under Assumption
1
, there exists a constant
\mu_{J}>0
such that the joint value function
J(x,\phi,\psi)
satisfies the following PŁ conditions for any
x
,
\phi
and
\psi
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu_{J}\left(J(x,\phi,\psi)-J_{2}(x,\psi)\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu_{J}\left(J_{1}(x,\phi)-J(x,\phi,\psi)\right).

Proof.

From Eq (
88
) in Lemma
C.5
, we have for any
x
and
\psi
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi})\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu^{\pi_{\phi}}(x,\phi)\left(V(x,\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi})-\min_{\phi^{\prime}}V(x,\pi_{\phi^{\prime}},\pi_{\psi})\right),

(99)

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu^{\pi_{\phi}}(x,\phi)\left(J(x,\phi,\psi)-J_{2}(x,\psi)\right),

(100)

where

\displaystyle\mu^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\phi)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{\phi}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho(s)\min_{s,a}\pi_{\phi}^{2}(a|s)\min_{s}\frac{d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)}{d^{{\dagger},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\tau_{\phi}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho(s)\left(\min_{s,a}\pi_{\phi}(a|s)\right)^{2}(1-\gamma)\min_{s}\rho(s)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\gamma)\frac{\min\{\tau_{\phi},\tau_{\psi}\}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\delta_{\rho}^{2}\delta_{\pi}^{2}.

(101)

Similarly, for any
x
and
\phi
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mu^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\psi)\left(J_{1}(x,\phi)-J(x,\phi,\psi)\right),

(102)

where

\displaystyle\mu^{\pi_{\psi}}(x,\psi)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau_{\psi}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho(s)\min_{s,b}\pi_{\psi}^{2}(b|s)\min_{s}\frac{d^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\rho}(s)}{d^{\pi_{\phi},{\dagger}}_{\rho}(s)}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\gamma)\frac{\min\{\tau_{\phi},\tau_{\psi}\}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\delta_{\rho}^{2}\delta_{\pi}^{2}.

(103)

Thus, by taking
\mu_{J}=(1-\gamma)\frac{\min\{\tau_{\phi},\tau_{\psi}\}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\delta_{\rho}^{2}\delta_{\pi}^{2}>0
, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma C.8
.

Under Assumption
1
, the NI function
g(x,\phi,\psi)
is
\mu_{g}
-PŁ in
(\phi,\psi)
for some constant
\mu_{g}>0
.

Proof.

By Lemma
C.6
and Assumption
1
, we have for any
x
and
\theta
,

\displaystyle\mu(x,\theta)

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma)\frac{\min\{\tau_{\phi},\tau_{\psi}\}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\min_{s}\rho^{2}(s)\min\{\min_{s,a}\pi^{2}_{\phi}(a|s),\min_{s,b}\pi^{2}_{\psi}(b|s)\}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\gamma)\frac{\min\{\tau_{\phi},\tau_{\psi}\}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\delta_{\rho}^{2}\delta_{\pi}^{2}.

(104)

Taking
\mu_{g}=(1-\gamma)\frac{\min\{\tau_{\phi},\tau_{\psi}\}}{|\mathcal{S}|}\delta_{\rho}^{2}\delta_{\pi}^{2}>0
, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma C.9
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, for any
x
and
\psi
,
define
\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi)\triangleq\arg\min_{\pi_{\phi}}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
.
Then there exists a constant
C_{\pi,1}
such that for any
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
,
\psi_{1}
, and
\psi_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|\leq C_{\pi,1}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right).

Similarly, for any
x
and
\phi
, define
\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)\triangleq\arg\max_{\pi_{\psi}}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
. Then there exists a constant
C_{\pi,2}
such that for any
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
,
\phi_{1}
, and
\phi_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|\leq C_{\pi,2}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|\right).

Proof.

By Lemma
C.4
, we know that
\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi)
is the unique optimal policy for a single-policy entropy-regularized MDP
\mathcal{M}^{\pi_{\psi}}(x)=\{\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},\widetilde{r}_{(x,\psi)},\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\psi},\gamma,\tau_{\phi}\}
for any given
x
and
\psi
, where
\widetilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}(s,a)
is defined as

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}(s,a)\triangleq-\mathbb{E}_{b\sim\psi(\cdot|s)}\left[r_{x}(s,a,b)-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b|s)\right],

and
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\psi}
is defined as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\psi}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{b\sim\psi(\cdot|s)}\left[\mathcal{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a,b)\right].

Moreover, we have the following properties for
\widetilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\psi}(s^{\prime}|s,a)
:

\displaystyle|\widetilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}(s,a)|\leq B_{r}+\tau_{\psi}\log|\mathcal{B}|,

(105)

and

\displaystyle\quad\,\sup_{s,a}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\psi_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)-\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\psi_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\sup_{s,a}\sum_{s^{\prime}}\left|\sum_{b}\pi_{\psi_{1}}(b|s)\mathcal{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a,b)-\pi_{\psi_{2}}(b|s)\mathcal{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a,b)\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{s,a}\sum_{s^{\prime}}\left|\sum_{b}(\pi_{\psi_{1}}(b|s)-\pi_{\psi_{2}}(b|s))\mathcal{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a,b)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,a}\sum_{b}|\pi_{\psi_{1}}(b|s)-\pi_{\psi_{2}}(b|s)|\sum_{s^{\prime}}\mathcal{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a,b)

\displaystyle=\sup_{s}\|\pi_{\psi_{1}}(\cdot|s)-\pi_{\psi_{2}}(\cdot|s)\|_{1},

(106)

and

\displaystyle\quad\,\sup_{s,a}|\widetilde{r}_{(x_{1},\psi_{1})}(s,a)-\widetilde{r}_{(x_{2},\psi_{2})}(s,a)|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,a}\left|\sum_{b}\pi_{\psi}(b|s)\left(r_{x_{1}}(s,a,b)-r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right)\right|+\sup_{s,a}\left|\sum_{b}\left(\pi_{\psi_{1}}(b|s)-\pi_{\psi_{2}}(b|s)\right)r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right|

\displaystyle\quad\;+\sup_{s,a}\left|\tau_{\psi}\left(H(\pi_{\psi_{1}}(\cdot|s))-H(\pi_{\psi_{2}}(\cdot|s))\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+B_{r}\sup_{s}\|\pi_{\psi_{1}}(\cdot|s)-\pi_{\psi_{2}}(\cdot|s)\|_{1}+\tau_{\psi}\sup_{s}|H(\pi_{\psi_{1}}(\cdot|s))-H(\pi_{\psi_{2}}(\cdot|s))|.

(107)

For any
s\in\mathcal{S}
, any
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\in\Delta_{n}
and
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\geq\delta_{\pi}\mathbf{1}
, the entropy function
H(\cdot)
is Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant
H_{\delta_{\pi}}=\max\{1,|1+\log\delta_{\pi}|\}
, which means

\displaystyle|H(\pi_{\psi_{1}}(\cdot|s))-H(\pi_{\psi_{2}}(\cdot|s))|\leq H_{\delta_{\pi}}\|\pi_{\psi_{1}}(\cdot|s)-\pi_{\psi_{2}}(\cdot|s)\|_{1}.

(108)

And we also have for softmax parameterized policy
\pi_{\psi}
and any state
s
,

\displaystyle\|\pi_{\psi_{1}}(\cdot|s)-\pi_{\psi_{2}}(\cdot|s)\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{B}|}\|\pi_{\psi_{1}}(\cdot|s)-\pi_{\psi_{2}}(\cdot|s)\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{B}|}}{2}\|\psi_{1}(s)-\psi_{2}(s)\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{B}|}}{2}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|.

(109)

Then we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\,\sup_{s,a}|\widetilde{r}_{(x_{1},\psi_{1})}(s,a)-\widetilde{r}_{(x_{2},\psi_{2})}(s,a)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{B}|}}{2}\left(B_{r}+\tau_{\psi}H_{\delta_{\pi}}\right)\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|+C_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}_{r}\|(x_{1},\psi_{1})-(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|,

(110)

where
\widetilde{C}_{r}=\sqrt{2}\max\{C_{r},\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{B}|}}{2}(B_{r}+\tau_{\psi}H_{\delta_{\pi}})\}
,
and

\displaystyle\sup_{s,a}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\psi_{1}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)-\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\psi_{2}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\|_{1}\leq\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{B}|}}{2}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{B}|}}{2}\|(x_{1},\psi_{1})-(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|.

(111)

Now we will show that
\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi)
is Lipschitz continuous in
\psi
. Let
Q^{*}(x,\psi)\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{A}|}
be the optimal soft Q-value for the MDP
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}(x,\psi)
. Since the assumptions required for Lemma
C.1
are satisfied, by Lemma
C.1
, there exists a constant
C_{Q}
such that for any
x
and
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|Q^{*}(x,\psi_{1})-Q^{*}(x,\psi_{2})\|\leq C_{Q}\|(x,\psi_{1})-(x,\psi_{2})\|.

And the optimal policy
\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi)
can be represented as

\displaystyle\pi_{\phi}^{*}(a|s;x,\psi)=\frac{\exp(Q^{*}_{sa}(x,\psi)/\tau_{\phi})}{\sum_{a^{\prime}}\exp(Q^{*}_{sa^{\prime}}(x,\psi)/\tau_{\phi})},\quad\forall s\in\mathcal{S},a\in\mathcal{A}.

Therefore, we have for any
x
and
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\quad\,\|\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\tau_{\phi}}\|Q^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-Q^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{Q}}{2\tau_{\phi}}\|(x_{1},\psi_{1})-(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{Q}}{2\tau_{\phi}}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right),

(112)

which completes the proof for the first part.
The second part can be proved similarly for the opponent policy
\psi
.
∎

Lemma C.10
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, for any
\phi,\psi
, define
\phi^{*}(x,\psi)=\arg\min_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)
and
\psi^{*}(x,\phi)=\arg\max_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)
, then we have for any
x_{1},x_{2},\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
, there exists a constant
C_{\pi}>0
such that

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}\left(\phi^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1}),\phi^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\right)\leq C_{\pi}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right),

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}\left(\psi^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1}),\psi^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\right)\leq C_{\pi}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|\right).

Proof.

Since we know that for any given
x
and
\psi
,
\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi)=\arg\min_{\pi_{\phi}}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
is unique, then we have

\displaystyle\phi^{*}(x,\psi)=\{\phi:\pi_{\phi}=\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi)\}=\{\log\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x,\psi)+\diag(c)\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{A}|}:c\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}\}.

Here we consider
\log\pi
and
\phi
as a long vector in
\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
, and
\mathbf{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
is a vector with all entries being
1
.
Thus, for any
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
,
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
, the distance between the two sets
\phi^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1})
and
\phi^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})
can be calculated as

\displaystyle\quad\,\dist\left(\phi^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1}),\phi^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\right)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\phi_{1}\in\phi^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1}),\phi_{2}\in\phi^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|

\displaystyle=\inf_{c_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|},c_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}}\|\log\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1})+\diag(c_{1})\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{A}|}-\log\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})-\diag(c_{2})\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{A}|}\|

\displaystyle=\inf_{c\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}}\|\log\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-\log\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})+\diag(c)\mathbf{1}_{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{A}|}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\log\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-\log\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\delta_{\pi}}\|\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-\pi_{\phi}^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\pi,1}}{\delta_{\pi}}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right).

(113)

Similarly, we can prove that for any
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
,
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}\left(\psi^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1}),\psi^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\right)\leq\frac{C_{\pi,2}}{\delta_{\pi}}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|\right).

(114)

By taking
C_{\pi}=\max\left\{\frac{C_{\pi,1}}{\delta_{\pi}},\frac{C_{\pi,2}}{\delta_{\pi}}\right\}
, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma C.11
.

Under Assumption
2
, the approximated gap
function
\tilde{g}(x,\phi,\psi,\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\psi})
is

C_{\widetilde{g}}
-Lipschitz continuous,
L_{\widetilde{g},1}
-smooth, and
has an
L_{\widetilde{g},2}
-Lipschitz continuous Hessian, for some constants

C_{\widetilde{g}}>0
,
L_{\widetilde{g},1}>0
, and

L_{\widetilde{g},2}>0
.

Proof.

Since the approximated gap function
\tilde{g}(x,\phi,\psi,\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\psi})
is defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(x,\phi,\psi,\tilde{\phi},\tilde{\psi})=J(x,\phi,\tilde{\psi})-J(x,\tilde{\phi},\psi),

(115)

then by Assumption
2
,
J(x,\phi,\psi)
is
C_{J}
-Lipschitz continuous,
L_{J,1}
-Lipschitz smooth and
L_{J,2}
-Hessian Lipschitz continuous in
(x,\phi,\psi)
, thus
the proof is straightforward by taking
C_{\widetilde{g}}=2C_{J}
,
L_{\widetilde{g},1}=2L_{J,1}
and
L_{\widetilde{g},2}=2L_{J,2}
.
∎

Lemma C.12

(Lemma
2
in the main text)
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, the gap function
g(x,\phi,\psi)
is
C_{g}
-Lipschitz continuous,
L_{g,1}
-Lipschitz smooth, and
L_{g,2}
-Hessian Lipschitz continuous in
(x,\phi,\psi)
, for some constants
C_{g}
,
L_{g,1}
and
L_{g,2}
.

Proof.

By Assumption
2
, we know that
J(x,\phi,\psi)
is
C_{J}
-Lipschitz continuous,
L_{J,1}
-Lipschitz smooth and
L_{J,2}
-Hessian Lipschitz continuous in
(x,\phi,\psi)
.
Then for any
x_{1},x_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
,

\displaystyle|J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi_{1})-J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{J}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right),

\displaystyle\|\nabla J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi_{1})-\nabla J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{J,1}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right),

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi_{1})-\nabla^{2}J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{J,2}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right).

(116)

Consequently, we can obtain

\displaystyle\quad\;J_{1}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-J_{1}(x_{2},\phi_{2})

\displaystyle=\max_{\psi_{1}}J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi_{1})-\max_{\psi_{2}}J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi_{2})

\displaystyle=J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi^{\prime})-\max_{\psi_{2}}J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi_{2}),\quad\psi^{\prime}\in\psi^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1})

\displaystyle\leq J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi^{\prime})-J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq C_{J}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|\right),

(117)

and similarly

\displaystyle\quad\;J_{2}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-J_{2}(x_{2},\psi_{2})

\displaystyle=\min_{\phi_{1}}J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi_{1})-\min_{\phi_{2}}J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi_{2})

\displaystyle\leq J(x_{1},\phi^{\prime},\psi_{1})-J(x_{2},\phi^{\prime},\psi_{2}),\quad\phi^{\prime}\in\phi^{*}(x_{2},\psi_{2})

\displaystyle\leq C_{J}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right).

(118)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\quad\;|g(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi_{1})-g(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi_{2})|

\displaystyle=|J_{1}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-J_{2}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-(J_{1}(x_{2},\phi_{2})-J_{2}(x_{2},\psi_{2}))|

\displaystyle\leq|J_{1}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-J_{1}(x_{2},\phi_{2})|+|J_{2}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-J_{2}(x_{2},\psi_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{J}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right),

(119)

which proves the Lipschitz continuity of
g(x,\phi,\psi)
.

Next, we will prove the Lipschitz smoothness of
g(x,\phi,\psi)
.
Since we know that for any
x
and
\phi
,
\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)=\arg\max_{\pi_{\psi}}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)
exists and is unique. By Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\;\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J_{1}(x,\phi)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\max_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\max_{\pi_{\psi}}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{(x,\phi)}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)\bigg|_{\pi_{\psi}=\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{(x,\phi)}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)\bigg|_{\psi\in\psi^{*}(x,\phi)}.

Since any
\psi\in\psi^{*}(x,\phi)
induces the same optimal policy
\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)
, then we have for any
\psi^{\prime},\psi^{\prime\prime}\in\psi^{*}(x,\phi)
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{(x,\phi)}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)\bigg|_{\psi=\psi^{\prime}}=\nabla_{(x,\phi)}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)\bigg|_{\pi_{\psi}=\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)}=\nabla_{(x,\phi)}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)\bigg|_{\psi=\psi^{\prime\prime}}.

Thus we can use any
\psi\in\psi^{*}(x,\phi)
to calculate
\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J_{1}(x,\phi)
, and we choose
\psi=\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)
, which is the canonical parameterization of the optimal policy
\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)
, then we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J_{1}(x,\phi)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{(x,\phi)}V^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(\rho)\bigg|_{\psi=\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)}=\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J(x,\phi,\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)),

which implies that for any
x_{1},x_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\quad\,\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J_{1}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J_{1}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|

\displaystyle=\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\log\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x_{1},\phi_{1}))-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\log\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x_{2},\phi_{2}))\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{J,1}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|+\|\log\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\log\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq L_{J,1}(1+C_{\pi})\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|\right).

(120)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.10
.

Similarly, we can prove that for any
x_{1},x_{2},\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{(x,\psi)}J_{2}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-\nabla_{(x,\psi)}J_{2}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{J,1}(1+C_{\pi})\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right).

(121)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\|\nabla g(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi_{1})-\nabla g(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\nabla_{x}J_{1}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\nabla_{x}J_{2}(x_{1},\psi_{1})\\
\nabla_{\phi}J_{1}(x_{1},\phi_{1})\\
-\nabla_{\psi}J_{2}(x_{1},\psi_{1})\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}\nabla_{x}J_{1}(x_{2},\phi_{2})-\nabla_{x}J_{2}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\\
\nabla_{\phi}J_{1}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\\
-\nabla_{\psi}J_{2}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\end{bmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J_{1}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}J_{1}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|+\|\nabla_{(x,\psi)}J_{2}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-\nabla_{(x,\psi)}J_{2}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{J,1}(1+C_{\pi})\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right),

(122)

which proves the Lipschitz smoothness of
g(x,\phi,\psi)
.

Finally, we will prove the Hessian Lipschitz continuity of
g(x,\phi,\psi)
.
By the chain rule, we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}J_{1}(x,\phi)

\displaystyle=\nabla^{2}_{11}J(x,\phi,\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi))+\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)\nabla^{2}_{12}J(x,\phi,\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)),

(123)

where
\nabla^{2}_{11}J(x,\phi,\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi))\in\mathbb{R}^{(d_{x}+|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|)\times(d_{x}+|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|)}
is the Hessian of
J
with respect to the first two arguments
(x,\phi)
, and
\nabla^{2}_{12}J(x,\phi,\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi))\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{B}|\times(d_{x}+|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|)}
is the cross derivative of
J
with respect to
(x,\phi)
and
\psi
, and
\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)\in\mathbb{R}^{(d_{x}+|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|)\times|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{B}|}
is the Jacobian of
\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)
with respect to
(x,\phi)
.

Since given any
x
and
\phi
,
\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)
is the optimal policy for the MDP
\mathcal{M}^{\pi_{\phi}}(x)
defined in the proof of Lemma
C.5
, where the reward function
\tilde{r}_{(x,\phi)}(s,b)
is defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{(x,\phi)}(s,b)=\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s)}\left[r_{x}(s,a,b)\right]-\tau_{\phi}H\left(\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s)\right),

and the transition probability
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\pi_{\phi}}(s^{\prime}|s,b)
is defined as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\pi_{\phi}}(s^{\prime}|s,b)=\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\phi}(\cdot|s)}\left[\mathcal{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a,b)\right].

and in the proof of Lemma
C.9
, we also show that there exist constants
\tilde{B}_{r}
,
\tilde{C}_{r}
and
\tilde{C}_{P}
such that the following conditions are satisfied:

\displaystyle|\tilde{r}_{(x,\psi)}(s,b)|

\displaystyle\leq B_{r}+\tau_{\phi}\log|\mathcal{A}|\triangleq\tilde{B}_{r},

(124)

\displaystyle\sup_{s,b}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\pi_{\phi_{1}}}(s^{\prime}|s,b)-\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\pi_{\phi_{2}}}(s^{\prime}|s,b)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{P}\|(x_{1},\phi_{1})-(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|,

(125)

\displaystyle\sup_{s,b}|\tilde{r}_{(x_{1},\phi_{1})}(s,b)-\tilde{r}_{(x_{2},\phi_{2})}(s,b)|

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{r}\|(x_{1},\phi_{1})-(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|.

(126)

Moreover, we also have

\displaystyle\quad\;\sup_{s,b}\left\|\nabla_{x}\tilde{r}_{(x_{1},\phi_{1})}(s,b)-\nabla_{x}\tilde{r}_{(x_{2},\phi_{2})}(s,b)\right\|

\displaystyle=\sup_{s,b}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\phi_{1}}(\cdot|s)}\left[\nabla_{x}r_{x_{1}}(s,a,b)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\phi_{2}}(\cdot|s)}\left[\nabla_{x}r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,b}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\phi_{1}}(\cdot|s)}\left[\nabla_{x}r_{x_{1}}(s,a,b)-\nabla_{x}r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\sup_{s,b}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\phi_{1}}(\cdot|s)}\left[\nabla_{x}r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{a\sim\pi_{\phi_{2}}(\cdot|s)}\left[\nabla_{x}r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+C_{r}\sup_{s}\|\pi_{\phi_{1}}(\cdot|s)-\pi_{\phi_{2}}(\cdot|s)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq L_{r}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+C_{r}\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}|}}{2}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|,

(127)

and

\displaystyle\quad\,\sup_{s,b}\left\|\nabla_{\phi}\tilde{r}_{(x_{1},\phi_{1})}(s,b)-\nabla_{\phi}\tilde{r}_{(x_{2},\phi_{2})}(s,b)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,b}\left\|\sum_{a}\nabla_{\phi}\pi_{\phi_{1}}(a|s)r_{x_{1}}(s,a,b)-\sum_{a}\nabla_{\phi}\pi_{\phi_{2}}(a|s)r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau_{\phi}\sup_{s}\left\|\nabla_{\phi}H(\pi_{\phi_{1}}(\cdot|s))-\nabla_{\phi}H(\pi_{\phi_{2}}(\cdot|s))\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s,b}\left\|\sum_{a}\nabla_{\phi}\pi_{\phi_{1}}(a|s)\left(r_{x_{1}}(s,a,b)-r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\sup_{s,b}\left\|\sum_{a}\left(\nabla_{\phi}\pi_{\phi_{1}}(a|s)-\nabla_{\phi}\pi_{\phi_{2}}(a|s)\right)r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau_{\phi}\sup_{s}\left\|\nabla_{\phi}H(\pi_{\phi_{1}}(\cdot|s))-\nabla_{\phi}H(\pi_{\phi_{2}}(\cdot|s))\right\|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{A}|\sup_{s,a,b}\left|r_{x_{1}}(s,a,b)-r_{x_{2}}(s,a,b)\right|+B_{r}|\mathcal{A}|\sup_{s,a}\|\nabla_{\phi}\pi_{\phi_{1}}(a|s)-\nabla_{\phi}\pi_{\phi_{2}}(a|s)\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tau_{\phi}\sup_{s}\left\|\nabla_{\phi}H(\pi_{\phi_{1}}(\cdot|s))-\nabla_{\phi}H(\pi_{\phi_{2}}(\cdot|s))\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(|\mathcal{A}|C_{r}+\frac{3}{2}B_{r}|\mathcal{A}|+\tau_{\phi}L_{H}\right)\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|,

(128)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma 6 in
53
, and
L_{H}=\frac{4+8\log|\mathcal{A}|}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}
.

Then there exists a constant
\widetilde{L}_{r}=\max\{L_{r},|\mathcal{A}|C_{r}+\frac{3}{2}B_{r}|\mathcal{A}|+\tau_{\phi}L_{H}\}
such that for any
x_{1},x_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{s,b}\left\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\tilde{r}_{(x_{1},\phi_{1})}(s,b)-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\tilde{r}_{(x_{2},\phi_{2})}(s,b)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{L}_{r}\|(x_{1},\phi_{1})-(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|.

(129)

For the transition probability, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\sup_{s,b}\left\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\pi_{\phi_{1}}}-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{\pi_{\phi_{2}}}\right\|

\displaystyle=\sup_{s,b}\left\|\sum_{a}\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\pi_{\phi_{1}}(a|s)\mathcal{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a,b)-\sum_{a}\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\pi_{\phi_{2}}(a|s)\mathcal{P}(s^{\prime}|s,a,b)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}|\mathcal{A}|\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}|\mathcal{A}|\|(x_{1},\phi_{1})-(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\triangleq\widetilde{C}_{P}\|(x_{1},\phi_{1})-(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|.

(130)

Then by Lemma
C.3
, we know that
\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\pi^{*}_{\psi}(x,\phi)
is well-defined and there exists a constant
L_{\psi,1}
such that for any
x_{1},x_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{\psi,1}\|(x_{1},\phi_{1})-(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|,

(131)

and by Lemma
C.2
, we know that there exists a constant
C_{\psi^{*}}
such that for any
x,\phi
,
\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x,\phi)\|\leq C_{\psi^{*}}
.

Therefore, we have for any
x_{1},x_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\quad\;\|\nabla^{2}J_{1}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\nabla^{2}J_{1}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla^{2}_{11}J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1}))-\nabla^{2}_{11}J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2}))\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1})\nabla^{2}_{12}J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1}))

\displaystyle\quad\,-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\nabla^{2}_{12}J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2}))\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{J,2}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|+\|\log\psi^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\log\psi^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|\cdot\|\nabla^{2}_{12}J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1}))\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|\cdot\|\nabla^{2}_{12}J(x_{1},\phi_{1},\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{1},\phi_{1}))-\nabla^{2}_{12}J(x_{2},\phi_{2},\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(x_{2},\phi_{2}))\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+L_{J,1}\sup_{s,b}\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(b|s;x_{1},\phi_{1})-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\log\pi_{\psi}^{*}(b|s;x_{2},\phi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{\psi^{*}}L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})+C_{\psi^{*}}L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})\right)\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+L_{J,1}\frac{1}{\delta_{\pi}}\sup_{s,b}\|\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\pi_{\psi}^{*}(b|s;x_{1},\phi_{1})-\nabla_{(x,\phi)}\pi_{\psi}^{*}(b|s;x_{2},\phi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})+C_{\psi^{*}}L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})+L_{J,1}\frac{L_{\psi,1}}{\delta_{\pi}}\right)\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|\right).

(132)

Thus, by taking
L_{J_{1},2}=L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})+C_{\psi^{*}}L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})+L_{J,1}\frac{L_{\psi,1}}{\delta_{\pi}}
, we prove the Hessian Lipschitz continuity of
J_{1}(x,\phi)
.

Similarly, we can prove that for any
x_{1},x_{2},\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}J_{2}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-\nabla^{2}J_{2}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{J_{2},2}\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right),

(133)

for some constant
L_{J_{2},2}=L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})+C_{\phi^{*}}L_{J,2}(1+C_{\pi})+L_{J,1}\frac{L_{\phi,1}}{\delta_{\pi}}
.

Therefore, we have for any
x_{1},x_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
,

\displaystyle\quad\,\|\nabla^{2}g(x_{1},\phi_{1},\psi_{1})-\nabla^{2}g(x_{2},\phi_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla^{2}J_{1}(x_{1},\phi_{1})-\nabla^{2}J_{1}(x_{2},\phi_{2})\|+\|\nabla^{2}J_{2}(x_{1},\psi_{1})-\nabla^{2}J_{2}(x_{2},\psi_{2})\|

\displaystyle\leq(L_{J_{1},2}+L_{J_{2},2})\left(\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\right),

(134)

which proves the Hessian Lipschitz continuity of
g(x,\phi,\psi)
.
∎

Lemma C.13
.

Under Assumptions
2
, let
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, then the penalized objective function
h(x,\phi,\psi)
is
L_{h,1}
-Lipschitz smooth for some constant
L_{h,1}>0
.

Proof.

By definition of
h(x,\phi,\psi)
, we have

\displaystyle h(x,\phi,\psi)=\frac{1}{\lambda}f(x,\phi,\psi)+g(x,\phi,\psi).

From Assumption
2
and Lemma
C.12
, for any
\lambda>0
, it is straightforward to verify that
h
is
\left(\frac{1}{\lambda}L_{f,1}+L_{g,1}\right)
-smooth. Therefore, we just need to take
L_{h,1}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}}L_{f,1}+L_{g,1}
.
∎

Lemma C.14
.

Under Assumption
1
and
2
, let
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, then
there exists a constant
L_{F}
such that
F(x)
is
L_{F}
-Lipschitz smooth.

Proof.

By Assumptions
1
and
2
, we have
f(x,\phi,\psi)
is
C_{f}
-Lipschitz,
L_{f,1}
-Lipschitz smooth and has
L_{f,2}
-Lipschitz Hessians in
(\phi,\psi)
.

By Lemma
C.12
, we also have
g(x,\phi,\psi)
has
L_{g,1}
-Lipschitz gradients and
L_{g,2}
-Lipschitz Hessians.

Moreover, by Assumption
2
and Lemma
C.8
,
cf(x,\phi,\psi)+g(x,\phi,\psi)
is
\mu_{h}
-PŁ in
(\phi,\psi)
for any
0\leq c\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}}
.

Therefore, by Lemma 4.4 in
8
, we know that there exists a constant
L_{F}
such that
F(x)
is
L_{F}
-Lipschitz smooth.

∎

Lemma C.15

(Estimation Error of Finite Horizon)
.

Under Assumption
1
and
2
, there exists a constant
\sigma_{H}>0
such that for any
x
,
\phi
,
\psi
, compared with the true gradient, the estimation error of the estimated gradient using horizon
H
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)-\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)-\nabla_{\psi}J(x,\phi,\psi)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{x}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)-\nabla_{x}J(x,\phi,\psi)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2H}\sigma_{H}^{2}.

Proof.

By Lemma
B.6
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\left(Q^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\right)\right].

(135)

Then expanding the Q-function, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})G_{t}\right],

(136)

where

\displaystyle G_{t}\triangleq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\gamma^{k-t}\left(r_{x}(s_{k},a_{k},b_{k})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{k}|s_{k})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{k}|s_{k})\right)\bigg|s_{0}=s_{t},a_{0}=a_{t},b_{0}=b_{t}\right].

(137)

If we use a finite horizon
H
, then the estimated gradient is

\displaystyle\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\widehat{G}_{t}^{H}\right],

(138)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{G}_{t}^{H}

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=t}^{H-1}\gamma^{k-t}\left(r_{x}(s_{k},a_{k},b_{k})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{k}|s_{k})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{k}|s_{k})\right)\bigg|s_{0}=s_{t},a_{0}=a_{t},b_{0}=b_{t}\right].

(139)

Then the bias is bounded as

\displaystyle\quad\,\left\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)-\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\widehat{G}_{t}^{H}-\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})G_{t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\widehat{G}_{t}^{H}-\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})G_{t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\;+\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})G_{t}\right]\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\left(\widehat{G}_{t}^{H}-G_{t}\right)\right]\right\|+\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})G_{t}\right]\right\|

(140)

Moreover, we have the following bounds:

\displaystyle|G_{t}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=t}^{\infty}\gamma^{k-t}\max_{s,a,b}\left(|r_{x}(s,a,b)|+\tau_{\psi}|\log\pi_{\psi}(b|s)|+\tau_{\phi}|\log\pi_{\phi}(a|s)|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B_{r}+(\tau_{\psi}+\tau_{\phi})\log\frac{1}{\delta_{\pi}}}{1-\gamma}\triangleq\frac{G_{\max}}{1-\gamma},

(141)

and

\displaystyle\quad\,|\widehat{G}_{t}^{H}-G_{t}|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{k-t}\left(r_{x}(s_{k},a_{k},b_{k})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{k}|s_{k})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{k}|s_{k})\right)\bigg|s_{0}=s_{t},a_{0}=a_{t},b_{0}=b_{t}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{H-t}\frac{G_{\max}}{1-\gamma},

(142)

and

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a|s)\|=\left\|\frac{\nabla_{\phi}\pi_{\phi}(a|s)}{\pi_{\phi}(a|s)}\right\|\leq\frac{C_{\pi}}{\delta_{\pi}}\triangleq G_{\pi}.

(143)

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\,\left\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)-\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\left(\widehat{G}_{t}^{H}-G_{t}\right)\right]\right\|^{2}+2\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})G_{t}\right]\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2G_{\pi}^{2}G_{\max}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\gamma^{2H}H^{2}+\frac{2G_{\pi}^{2}G_{\max}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\gamma^{2H}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4G_{\pi}^{2}G_{\max}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}\gamma^{2H}H^{2}.

(144)

Therefore, by taking
\sigma_{H}\geq\frac{2G_{\pi}G_{\max}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
, we complete the proof of the estimation error for the gradient estimator of
\phi
. The proof for the estimation error of the gradient estimator of
\psi
follows analogously.

The true gradient of
J(x,\phi,\psi)
with respect to
x
is

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}J(x,\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right],

(145)

and the estimated gradient with finite horizon
H
is

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right].

(146)

Then the estimation error of using finite horizon
H
is

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{x}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)-\nabla_{x}J(x,\phi,\psi)\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right]\right\|^{2}\leq C_{r}^{2}\left(\sum_{t=H}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\right)^{2}=C_{r}^{2}\frac{\gamma^{2H}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}

(147)

Therefore, by taking
\sigma_{H}=\max\{\frac{C_{r}}{1-\gamma},\frac{2G_{\pi}G_{\max}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\}>0
, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma C.16
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, in Algorithm
1
, for any
x
,
\phi
and
\psi
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;B_{J},H)-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;B_{J},H)-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\psi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;B_{J},H)-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\psi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}},

for some constant
