\sigma_{J}^{2}>0
.

Proof.

By Lemma
B.5
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}J(x,\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right],

thus the truncated gradient with horizon
H
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right]=\nabla_{x}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right],

where the first equality holds due to the unbiasedness of Monte Carlo sampling with horizon
H
.

The stochastic gradient estimator with batch size
B_{J}
is given by

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;B_{J},H)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B_{J}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{J}}\left(\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t}^{i},a_{t}^{i},b_{t}^{i})\right).

(148)

Then we can get

\displaystyle\quad\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;B_{J},H)-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{B_{J}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{J}}\left(\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t}^{i},a_{t}^{i},b_{t}^{i})-\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right]\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

(149)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B_{J}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})-\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

(150)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B_{J}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]-\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right]\right\|^{2}\right)

(151)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{B_{J}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla_{x}r_{x}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

(152)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{B_{J}}\frac{C_{r}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}},

(153)

where the second equality holds due to
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X]\right\|^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-\mathbb{E}[X]\right\|^{2}\right]
; the third equality holds due to
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|X-\mathbb{E}[X]\right\|^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\|X\|^{2}-\|\mathbb{E}[X]\|^{2}
; the last inequality follows from
C_{r}
-Lipschitz continuity of
r_{x}
.

By Lemma
B.6
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\nabla\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\left(Q^{\pi_{\phi},\pi_{\psi}}_{\mathcal{M}(x)}(s_{t},a_{t},b_{t})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{t}|s_{t})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\right)\right],

then the truncated gradient with horizon
H
is given by

\displaystyle\quad\,\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right]

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\phi}J(x,\phi,\psi;H)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})G_{t,H}\right],

(154)

where

\displaystyle G_{t,H}\triangleq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=t}^{H-1}\gamma^{k-t}\left(r_{x}(s_{k},a_{k},b_{k})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{k}|s_{k})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{k}|s_{k})\right)\bigg|s_{0}=s_{t},a_{0}=a_{t},b_{0}=b_{t}\right].

(155)

The stochastic gradient estimator with batch size
B_{J}
is given by

\displaystyle\quad\,\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;B_{J},H)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B_{J}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{J}}\left(\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}^{i}|s_{t}^{i})\widehat{G}_{t,H}^{i}\right),

(156)

where

\displaystyle\widehat{G}_{t,H}^{i}\triangleq\sum_{k=t}^{H-1}\gamma^{k-t}\left(r_{x}(s_{k}^{i},a_{k}^{i},b_{k}^{i})-\tau_{\psi}\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{k}^{i}|s_{k}^{i})+\tau_{\phi}\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{k}^{i}|s_{k}^{i})\right),

(157)

and
\{s_{t}^{i},a_{t}^{i},b_{t}^{i}\}_{t=0}^{H-1}
is the
i
th trajectory i.i.d. sampled under policies
\pi_{\phi}
and
\pi_{\psi}
in MDP
\mathcal{M}(x)
.

The upper bound of the absolute value of
\widehat{G}_{t,H}
is given by

\displaystyle|\widehat{G}_{t,H}^{i}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=t}^{H-1}\gamma^{k-t}\left(|r_{x}(s_{k}^{i},a_{k}^{i},b_{k}^{i})|+\tau_{\psi}|\log\pi_{\psi}(b_{k}^{i}|s_{k}^{i})|+\tau_{\phi}|\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{k}^{i}|s_{k}^{i})|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=t}^{H-1}\gamma^{k-t}G_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}G_{\max},

(158)

where
G_{\max}
is the upper bound of the absolute value of the reward and the entropy terms defined in Lemma
C.15
.

Then we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;B_{J},H)-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{B_{J}}\nabla_{\phi}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{J}}\hat{J}_{i}(x,\phi,\psi;H)-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

(159)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B_{J}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

(160)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{B_{J}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;H)\right\|^{2}\right]

(161)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B_{J}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\nabla\log\pi_{\phi}(a_{t}|s_{t})\widehat{G}_{t,H}\right\|^{2}\right]

(162)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{B_{J}}\frac{G_{\pi}^{2}G_{\max}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\left(\sum_{t=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{t}\right)^{2}

(163)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{B_{J}}\frac{G_{\pi}^{2}G_{\max}^{2}}{(1-\gamma)^{4}},

(164)

where (
159
)–(
161
)
are obtained using the same argument as
(
149
)–(
152
),
and the second-to-last inequality uses the definitions of
G_{\pi}
and

G_{\max}
in Lemma
C.15
. The proof for
\nabla_{\psi}\hat{J}(x,\phi,\psi;B_{J},H)
follows analogously.

Therefore, by taking
\sigma_{J}=\max\{\frac{C_{r}}{1-\gamma},\frac{G_{\pi}G_{\max}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\}>0
, we complete the proof.
∎

C.4
Proofs of Theorem
1

Lemma C.17
.

Suppose
1
to
3
hold.
Then, for each outer iteration
t
of
Algorithm
1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\ell_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{2\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}+\frac{8\lambda^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}.

Proof.

In Algorithm
1
, we have

\displaystyle\ell_{t}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}\widehat{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}_{\lambda}(x_{t},y_{t+1},z_{t+1},\tilde{y}_{t+1},\tilde{z}_{t+1};B,B_{J},H)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}\hat{f}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{K},\psi_{t}^{K};B)+\lambda\nabla_{x}\widehat{\tilde{g}}(x_{t},y_{t+1},z_{t+1},\tilde{y}_{t+1},\tilde{z}_{t+1};B_{J},H)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}\hat{f}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{K},\psi_{t}^{K};B)+\lambda\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{K},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{K};B_{J},H)-\lambda\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{K},\psi_{t}^{K};B_{J},H).

(165)

Therefore, it follows from Assumption
3
and Lemma
C.16
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\ell_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{B}\sum_{i=1}^{B}\nabla_{x}\hat{f}_{i}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{K},\psi_{t}^{K})-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{x}\hat{f}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{K},\psi_{t}^{K})\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+4\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{K},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{K};B_{J},H)-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{K},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{K};H)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+4\lambda^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{K},\psi_{t}^{K};B_{J},H)-\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla_{x}\hat{J}(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{K},\psi_{t}^{K};H)\right]\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}+\frac{8\lambda^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}.

(166)

∎

Lemma C.18
.

Suppose
1
to
3
hold.
In Algorithm
1
, if
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\quad\,\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{T}\frac{8L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\Delta_{\theta}+C_{1}\frac{8L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathcal{O}(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{B_{J}})+\mathcal{O}(\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B})+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}H^{2}),

where
C_{1}=\frac{1}{c}\left(2L_{f,1}^{2}+32\lambda^{2}L_{J,1}^{2}+32\lambda^{2}L_{J,1}^{2}C_{\pi}^{2}\right)
,
c
is a constant defined in (
189
),
L_{\theta}=\max\{L_{h,1},L_{J,1}\}
,
\mu_{\theta}=\min\{\mu_{h},\mu_{J}\}
, and
\Delta_{\theta}
is the initial optimality gap of the parameters defined in (
206
).

Proof.

In this proof, we use the notation for
\theta
,
\theta^{*}(x)
, and

\theta^{*}(x,\theta)
introduced in Table
A.1
.

In Algorithm
1
, the estimation error is bounded as

\displaystyle\quad\,\left\|\mathbb{E}\left[\ell_{t}\right]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\nabla f(x_{t},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t}))-\nabla f(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K})\|^{2}+2\lambda^{2}\|\nabla\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t}),\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta^{*}_{\lambda}(x_{t})))-\nabla\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{K};H)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{f,1}^{2}\dist^{2}(\theta_{t}^{K},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t}))+4\lambda^{2}\|\nabla\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t}),\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta^{*}_{\lambda}(x_{t})))-\nabla\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{K})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\,+4\lambda^{2}\|\nabla\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{K})-\nabla\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{K};H)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{f,1}^{2}\dist^{2}(\theta_{t}^{K},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t}))+2\lambda^{2}L_{\widetilde{g},1}^{2}\left(2\dist^{2}(\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t}),\theta_{t}^{K})+2\dist^{2}(\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})),\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{K})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+8\lambda^{2}\|\nabla_{x}J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{K},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{K})-\nabla_{x}J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{K},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{K};H)\|^{2}+8\lambda^{2}\|\nabla_{x}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{K},\psi_{t}^{K})-\nabla_{x}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{K},\psi_{t}^{K};H)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2L_{f,1}^{2}+4\lambda^{2}L_{\widetilde{g},1}^{2}\right)\dist^{2}(\theta^{*}_{\lambda}(x_{t}),\theta_{t}^{K})+4\lambda^{2}L_{\widetilde{g},1}^{2}\dist^{2}(\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})),(\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{K},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{K}))+16\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}\sigma_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2L_{f,1}^{2}+4\lambda^{2}L_{\widetilde{g},1}^{2}+8\lambda^{2}L_{\widetilde{g},1}^{2}C_{\pi}^{2}\right)\dist^{2}(\theta^{*}_{\lambda}(x_{t}),\theta_{t}^{K})+8\lambda^{2}L_{\widetilde{g},1}^{2}\dist^{2}(\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K}),\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{K})+16\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}\sigma_{H}^{2},

(167)

where the second inequality decomposes the estimation error of
\tilde{g}
into an optimization error and a stochastic error arising from the finite horizon
H
; the second-to-last inequality is implied by Lemma
C.15
; and the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.10
.

We next bound the distance terms arising from the inner loop. By the
L_{h,1}
-smoothness of
h(x,\theta)
, we have for
\eta_{\theta}\leq\frac{1}{2L_{h,1}}
,

\displaystyle h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})+\langle\nabla_{\theta}h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k}),\theta_{t}^{k+1}-\theta_{t}^{k}\rangle+\frac{L_{h,1}}{2}\|\theta_{t}^{k+1}-\theta_{t}^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})\|^{2}+\left(\frac{L_{h,1}\eta_{\theta}^{2}}{2}-\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\right)\|g_{t}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\|b_{t}^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})\|^{2}-\frac{1}{4\eta_{\theta}}\|\theta_{t}^{k+1}-\theta_{t}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\|b_{t}^{k}\|^{2},

(168)

where
b_{t}^{k}\triangleq\nabla_{\theta}h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-g_{t}^{k}
is the gradient-estimation error.

Since
h(x,\theta)
satisfies the
\mu_{h}
-PŁ condition with respect to

\theta
, it follows that

\displaystyle h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k+1})-h^{*}(x_{t})

\displaystyle\leq h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-h^{*}(x_{t})-\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\|\nabla_{\theta}h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})\|^{2}-\frac{1}{4\eta_{\theta}}\|\theta_{t}^{k+1}-\theta_{t}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\|b_{t}^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}\right)\left(h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-h^{*}(x_{t})\right)-\frac{1}{4\eta_{\theta}}\|\theta_{t}^{k+1}-\theta_{t}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\|b_{t}^{k}\|^{2},

(169)

where
h^{*}(x_{t})=\min_{\theta}h(x_{t},\theta)
.

For the error term
b_{t}^{k}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|b_{t}^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla_{\theta}h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-g_{t}^{k}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla_{\theta}f(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-\frac{1}{\lambda}\nabla_{\theta}\hat{f}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k};B)+\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k}))-\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\tilde{g}}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1};B_{J},H)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\lambda^{2}}\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}+4\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k}))-\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\,+4\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1})-\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\tilde{g}}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1};B_{J},H)\right\|^{2},

(170)

where the second equality follows from
g(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})=\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k}))
and the corresponding gradient identity with respect to
\theta
.

We first consider the second error term between
\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}
and
\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\tilde{g}}(\cdot;B_{J},H)
, which arises from the stochastic gradient estimation with batch size
B_{J}
and the finite-horizon truncation with horizon length
H
. We have

\displaystyle\quad\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1})-\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\tilde{g}}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1};B_{J},H)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1})-\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\tilde{g}}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1};H)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\tilde{g}}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1};H)-\frac{1}{B_{J}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{J}}\nabla_{\theta}\widehat{\tilde{g}}_{i}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1};H)\right\|^{2}\right]

(171)

\displaystyle\leq 4\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{4\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}},

(172)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.15
and Lemma
C.16
.

The first error term can be bounded as

\displaystyle\quad\,\|\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k}))-\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}\dist(\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k}),\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1})^{2}

(173)

\displaystyle=L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}\dist^{2}(\phi^{*}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k}),\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1})+L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}\dist^{2}(\psi^{*}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k}),\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)+\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}\left(-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k+1})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\right),

(174)

where the first inequality follows from the
L_{\widetilde{g},1}
-smoothness of
\tilde{g}
in Lemma
C.11
; the second inequality follows from the QG condition implied by the
\mu_{J}
-PŁ condition of
J(x,\phi,\psi)
in Lemma
C.7
.

We next bound the optimality gap of
J
. By the
L_{J,1}
-smoothness of
J(x,\phi,\psi)
and
\mu_{J}
-PŁ of
J(x,\phi,\psi)
in
\phi
, we have for
\eta_{\phi}\leq\frac{1}{L_{J,1}}
,

\displaystyle\quad\,J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})

\displaystyle\leq J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})+\langle\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k}),\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1}-\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k}\rangle+\frac{L_{J,1}}{2}\|\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1}-\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})-\frac{\eta_{\phi}}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left(\frac{L_{J,1}\eta_{\phi}^{2}}{2}-\frac{\eta_{\phi}}{2}\right)\|u_{t}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{\phi}}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-u_{t}^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})-\frac{\eta_{\phi}}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{\phi}}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-u_{t}^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\phi}\right)\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)+\frac{\eta_{\phi}}{2}\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-u_{t}^{k}\|^{2},

(175)

where the first inequality follows from the
L_{J,1}
-smoothness of
J(x,\phi,\psi)
; the first equality follows from the update rule of
\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k}
and the identity

\langle a,b\rangle=\tfrac{1}{2}\|a\|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|b\|^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|a-b\|^{2};

the second inequality follows from
\eta_{\phi}\leq\tfrac{1}{L_{J,1}}
; and the last inequality follows from the
\mu_{J}
-PŁ condition of
J(x,\phi,\psi)
.

The error term can be bounded as

\displaystyle\quad\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-u_{t}^{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k};B_{J},H)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k};H)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla_{\phi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k};H)-\frac{1}{B_{J}}\sum_{i=1}^{B_{J}}\nabla_{\phi}\hat{J}_{i}(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k};H)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{2\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}},

(176)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.15
and Lemma
C.16
.

Therefore, the optimality gap of
J
w.r.t.
\phi
can be bounded by

\displaystyle J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\leq\left(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\phi}\right)\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)+\eta_{\phi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right).

(177)

Similarly, for
\eta_{\psi}\leq\frac{1}{L_{J,1}}
, we can bound the optimality gap of
J
w.r.t.
\psi
by

\displaystyle-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k+1})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\leq\left(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\psi}\right)\left(-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\right)+\eta_{\psi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right).

(178)

Thus, substituting the above two inequalities back into (
174
), we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\|\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k}))-\nabla_{\theta}\tilde{g}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k},\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{k+1})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)+\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}\left(-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k+1})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\phi})\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)+\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\psi})\left(-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}\left(\eta_{\phi}+\eta_{\psi}\right)\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\phi})\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)+\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\psi})\left(-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{16L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{b}\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\right)+\frac{16L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right),

(179)

where
\kappa_{b}\triangleq\max\left\{\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\phi}),\frac{2L^{2}_{\widetilde{g},1}}{\mu_{J}}(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\psi})\right\}
, and the second-to-last inequality follows from
L_{\widetilde{g},1}=2L_{J,1}
in Lemma
C.11
and
\eta_{\phi}\leq\frac{1}{L_{J,1}}
,
\eta_{\psi}\leq\frac{1}{L_{J,1}}
.

Combining the preceding estimates yields the following bound on the error term
b_{t}^{k}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|b_{t}^{k}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{b}\mathbb{E}\bigg(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\,+16\left(1+\frac{L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)+\frac{2\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\lambda^{2}}.

(180)

Moreover, by the
L_{J,1}
-smoothness of
J(x,\phi,\psi)
, and
L_{J_{2},1}
-smoothness of
J_{2}(x,\psi)
established in Lemma
C.12
, where
L_{J_{2},1}
is absorbed into the NI smoothness constant
L_{g,1}
, we have, for
\eta_{\theta}\leq 1/(L_{J,1}+L_{J_{2},1})
,

\displaystyle\quad\,J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k+1})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})+\langle\nabla_{\psi}J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-\nabla_{\psi}J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k}),\psi_{t}^{k+1}-\psi_{t}^{k}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{L_{J,1}+L_{J_{2},1}}{2}\|\psi_{t}^{k+1}-\psi_{t}^{k}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})+\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}\dist(\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\phi^{*}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k}))\|\frac{\psi^{k+1}_{t}-\psi^{k}_{t}}{\eta_{\theta}}\|

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{(L_{J,1}+L_{J_{2},1})\eta_{\theta}^{2}}{2}\|\frac{\psi^{k+1}_{t}-\psi^{k}_{t}}{\eta_{\theta}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{2}\dist^{2}(\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\phi^{*}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k}))

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left(\frac{\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{2\alpha}+\frac{(L_{J,1}+L_{J_{2},1})\eta_{\theta}^{2}}{2}\right)\|\frac{\psi^{k+1}_{t}-\psi^{k}_{t}}{\eta_{\theta}}\|^{2},\quad\forall\alpha>0

\displaystyle\leq J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left(\frac{\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{2\alpha}+\frac{(L_{J,1}+L_{J_{2},1})\eta_{\theta}^{2}}{2}\right)\|\frac{\psi^{k+1}_{t}-\psi^{k}_{t}}{\eta_{\theta}}\|^{2},\quad\forall\alpha>0

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left(\frac{\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{2\alpha}+\frac{(L_{J,1}+L_{J_{2},1})\eta_{\theta}^{2}}{2}\right)\|\frac{\psi^{k+1}_{t}-\psi^{k}_{t}}{\eta_{\theta}}\|^{2},\quad\forall\alpha>0,

(181)

where the second inequality is due to the
L_{J,1}
-smoothness of
J
;
the third inequality uses the fact that
ab\leq\frac{\alpha}{2}a^{2}+\frac{1}{2\alpha}b^{2}
for any
\alpha>0
; the last inequality follows from the
\mu_{J}
-PŁ condition of
J
.

Now substituting (
177
) into the above inequality, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k+1})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(1-\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J}\right)\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)+\left(\frac{\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{2\alpha}+\frac{(L_{J,1}+L_{J_{2},1})\eta_{\theta}^{2}}{2}\right)\|\frac{\psi^{k+1}_{t}-\psi^{k}_{t}}{\eta_{\theta}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\phi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right),\quad\forall\alpha>0.

(182)

Let

\displaystyle\kappa_{1,1}

\displaystyle\triangleq\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(1-\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa_{2,1}

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{L_{J,1}}{2\eta_{\theta}\alpha}+\frac{L_{J,1}+L_{J_{2},1}}{2},

where the definition of
\kappa_{2,1}
absorbs the factor
\eta_{\theta}^{-2}
from
\|(\psi_{t}^{k+1}-\psi_{t}^{k})/\eta_{\theta}\|^{2}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\quad\,J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k+1})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{1,1}\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)+\left(\frac{\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{2\alpha}+\frac{(L_{J,1}+L_{J_{2},1})\eta_{\theta}^{2}}{2}\right)\|\frac{\psi^{k+1}_{t}-\psi^{k}_{t}}{\eta_{\theta}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\phi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1,1}\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})\right)+\kappa_{2,1}\|\psi^{k+1}_{t}-\psi^{k}_{t}\|^{2}+\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\phi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right).

(183)

Similarly, by the
L_{J,1}
-smoothness of
J(x,\phi,\psi)
, and
L_{J_{1},1}
-smoothness of
J_{1}(x,\phi)
, we have for
\eta_{\theta}\leq 1/\left(L_{J,1}+L_{J_{1},1}\right)
and any
\alpha^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle\quad\,-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k+1},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k+1})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{1,2}\left(-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\right)+\left(\frac{\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{2\alpha^{\prime}}+\frac{(L_{J,1}+L_{J_{1},1})\eta_{\theta}^{2}}{2}\right)\|\frac{\phi^{k+1}_{t}-\phi^{k}_{t}}{\eta_{\theta}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\psi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1,2}\left(-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\right)+\kappa_{2,2}\|\phi^{k+1}_{t}-\phi^{k}_{t}\|^{2}+\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\psi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right),

(184)

where

\displaystyle\kappa_{1,2}

\displaystyle\triangleq\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(1-\eta_{\psi}\mu_{J}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa_{2,2}

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{L_{J,1}}{2\eta_{\theta}\alpha^{\prime}}+\frac{L_{J,1}+L_{J_{1},1}}{2}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k+1},\psi_{t}^{k+1})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k+1})-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k+1},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k+1})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k+1})

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{1}\left(J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k})\right)+\kappa_{2}\|\theta^{k+1}_{t}-\theta^{k}_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\phi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)+\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\psi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right),

(185)

where
\kappa_{1}=\max\{\kappa_{1,1},\kappa_{1,2}\}
and
\kappa_{2}=\max\{\kappa_{2,1},\kappa_{2,2}\}
.

Since our goal is to derive a joint recursion involving both
h
and
J
,
we multiply the preceding inequality by a positive constant
c>0
and add it
to (
169
). Define

J_{t}^{k}=J(x_{t},\tilde{\phi}_{t}^{k},\psi_{t}^{k})-J_{2}(x_{t},\psi_{t}^{k})-J(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k},\tilde{\psi}_{t}^{k})+J_{1}(x_{t},\phi_{t}^{k}).

Then we have

\displaystyle\quad\,h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k+1})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{k+1}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})\left(h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-h^{*}(x_{t})\right)+\left(c\kappa_{2}-\frac{1}{4\eta_{\theta}}\right)\|\theta_{t}^{k+1}-\theta_{t}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\|b_{t}^{k}\|^{2}+c\kappa_{1}J_{t}^{k}

\displaystyle\quad\,+c\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\phi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)+c\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\psi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right).

(186)

Taking expectation with respect to all sources of randomness and substituting
the bound on
b_{t}^{k}
from (
180
) into the
preceding inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\,\mathbb{E}\left[h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k+1})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{k+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})\mathbb{E}\left[h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-h^{*}(x_{t})\right]+\left(c\kappa_{1}+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\kappa_{b}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[J_{t}^{k}\right]+\left(c\kappa_{2}-\frac{1}{4\eta_{\theta}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\theta_{t}^{k+1}-\theta_{t}^{k}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+8\eta_{\theta}\left(1+\frac{L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\lambda^{2}}\right)+c\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\phi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+c\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\psi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right).

(187)

To obtain a contraction, it suffices to impose the following two conditions:

\displaystyle c\kappa_{2}-\frac{1}{4\eta_{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{cL_{J,1}}{2\eta_{\theta}\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}}+\frac{cL_{J,1}+cL_{J,\star}}{2}-\frac{1}{4\eta_{\theta}}

(188)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\eta_{\theta}}\left(c\left(\frac{L_{J,1}}{\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}}+L_{J,1}\eta_{\theta}+L_{J,\star}\eta_{\theta}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\right)\leq 0,

where
L_{J,\star}=\max\{L_{J_{1},1},L_{J_{2},1}\}
,
and

\displaystyle c\kappa_{1}+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\kappa_{b}<c(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}).

Since

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(1-\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J}\right),\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(1-\eta_{\psi}\mu_{J}\right)\right\},

and
\kappa_{b}=\max\left\{\frac{2L_{\widetilde{g},1}^{2}}{\mu_{J}}(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\phi}),\frac{2L_{\widetilde{g},1}^{2}}{\mu_{J}}(1-\mu_{J}\eta_{\psi})\right\}>0
is independent of
\eta_{\theta}
, it suffices to ensure that the following
three inequalities hold:

\displaystyle c\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(1-\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J}\right)+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\kappa_{b}-c(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})<0,

\displaystyle c\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(1-\eta_{\psi}\mu_{J}\right)+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\kappa_{b}-c(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})<0,

\displaystyle c\left(\frac{L_{J,1}}{\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}}+L_{J,1}\eta_{\theta}+L_{J,\star}\eta_{\theta}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Choose

\displaystyle\begin{cases}&\alpha=\frac{\mu_{J}}{L_{J,1}(1-\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J})}>0\\
&\alpha^{\prime}=\frac{\mu_{J}}{L_{J,1}(1-\eta_{\psi}\mu_{J})}>0\\
&c=\min\{\frac{1}{2\left(\frac{L_{J,1}}{\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}}+L_{J,1}+L_{J,\star}\right)},1\}>0\\
&\eta_{\theta}\leq\min\{(\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J})(2+2\mu_{h}+\frac{\kappa_{b}}{c})^{-1},(\eta_{\psi}\mu_{J})(2+2\mu_{h}+\frac{\kappa_{b}}{c})^{-1},\frac{1}{2L_{h,1}},\frac{1}{\mu_{h}+1},1\}\\
&\eta_{\phi}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{L_{J,1}},\frac{1}{\mu_{J}+1},1\}\\
&\eta_{\psi}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{L_{J,1}},\frac{1}{\mu_{J}+1},1\}\end{cases}.

(189)

With these choices,
c=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
\eta_{\theta}=\mathcal{O}(1)
,

\eta_{\phi}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, and
\eta_{\psi}=\mathcal{O}(1)
. Moreover, we obtain

\displaystyle c\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(1-\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J}\right)+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\kappa_{b}-c(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})=c\left(-\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J}+\eta_{\theta}\left(1+\frac{\kappa_{b}}{2c}+\mu_{h}\right)\right)\leq-c\frac{\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J}}{2}<0,

(190)

\displaystyle c\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(1-\eta_{\psi}\mu_{J}\right)+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{2}\kappa_{b}-c(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})=c\left(-\eta_{\psi}\mu_{J}+\eta_{\theta}\left(1+\frac{\kappa_{b}}{2c}+\mu_{h}\right)\right)\leq-c\frac{\eta_{\psi}\mu_{J}}{2}<0,

(191)

\displaystyle c\left(\frac{L_{J,1}}{\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}}+L_{J,1}\eta_{\theta}+L_{J,\star}\eta_{\theta}\right)\leq c\left(\frac{L_{J,1}}{\min\{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\}}+L_{J,1}+L_{J,\star}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2},

(192)

\displaystyle\max\{\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right),\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\}\leq\max\{1+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{1-\eta_{\phi}\mu_{J}},1+\frac{\eta_{\theta}}{1-\eta_{\psi}\mu_{J}}\}\leq 2.

(193)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\quad\;\mathbb{E}\left[h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k+1})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{k+1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})\mathbb{E}\left[h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{k}\right]+8\eta_{\theta}\left(1+\frac{L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\lambda^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+c\left(1+\frac{\alpha\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\phi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)+c\left(1+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{\theta}L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\eta_{\psi}\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})\mathbb{E}\left[h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{k})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{k}\right]+12\left(1+\frac{L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\lambda^{2}}\right),

(194)

where the last inequality follows from the parameter choices in (
189
) and the upper bound in (
193
).

Denote

\displaystyle E_{\theta}\triangleq\left(1+\frac{L_{J,1}}{\mu_{J}}\right)\left(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}+\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\lambda^{2}}\right)=\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{2H}H^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{B_{J}}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B\lambda^{2}}\right).

(195)

Here,
\sigma_{H}
and
\sigma_{J}
are constants from
Lemma
C.15

and Lemma
C.16
, respectively, while
\sigma_{f}
is the
variance bound in Assumption
3
.
Thus, only the dependence on
\sigma_{f}
is retained explicitly in the final
bound.

By unrolling the preceding inequality over
k=0,1,\ldots,K-1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\,\mathbb{E}\left[h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{K}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{0})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{0}\right]+12\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}\right)^{K-k-1}E_{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{0})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{0}\right]+\frac{12}{\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}.

(196)

For notational simplicity, we suppress the expectation notation whenever it is clear from context. Then we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\dist^{2}(\theta_{t}^{K},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t}))+c\dist^{2}(\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{K},\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K}))

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{2}{\mu_{h}}\left(h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K})-h^{*}(x_{t})\right)+c\frac{2}{\mu_{J}}J_{t}^{K}

(197)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\frac{2}{\mu_{\theta}}\left(h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{K}\right)

(198)

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}\frac{2}{\mu_{\theta}}(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})^{K}\bigg(h(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{0})-h^{*}(x_{t})+cJ_{t}^{0}\bigg)+\frac{24}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}

(199)

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\leq}\frac{2}{\mu_{\theta}}(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})^{K}L_{\theta}\left(\dist^{2}\left(\theta_{t}^{0},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right)+c\dist^{2}\left(\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{0},\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{0})\right)\right)+\frac{24}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}

(200)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\mu_{\theta}}(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})^{K}L_{\theta}\left(\dist^{2}\left(\theta_{t-1}^{K},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right)+c\dist^{2}\left(\tilde{\theta}_{t-1}^{K},\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t-1}^{K})\right)\right)+\frac{24}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}

(201)

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{\leq}\frac{4}{\mu_{\theta}}(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})^{K}L_{\theta}\left(\dist^{2}\left(\theta_{t-1}^{K},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t-1})\right)+c\dist^{2}\left(\tilde{\theta}_{t-1}^{K},\theta^{*}(x_{t-1},\theta_{t-1}^{K})\right)\right)+\frac{24}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{4}{\mu_{\theta}}(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})^{K}L_{\theta}\left(\dist^{2}\left(\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t}),\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t-1})\right)+c\dist^{2}\left(\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t-1}^{K}),\theta^{*}(x_{t-1},\theta_{t-1}^{K})\right)\right)

(202)

\displaystyle\overset{(f)}{\leq}\frac{4}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)L_{\theta}\left(\dist^{2}\left(\theta_{t-1}^{K},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t-1})\right)+c\dist^{2}\left(\tilde{\theta}_{t-1}^{K},\theta^{*}(x_{t-1},\theta_{t-1}^{K})\right)\right)+\frac{24}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{4}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)L_{\theta}\left(\frac{L_{h,1}^{2}}{\mu^{2}_{h}}+cC_{\pi}^{2}\right)\|x_{t}-x_{t-1}\|^{2}

(203)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\left(\dist^{2}\left(\theta_{t-1}^{K},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t-1})\right)+c\dist^{2}\left(\tilde{\theta}_{t-1}^{K},\theta^{*}(x_{t-1},\theta_{t-1}^{K})\right)\right)+\frac{24}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\left(\frac{L_{\theta}^{2}}{\mu^{2}_{\theta}}+C_{\pi}^{2}\right)\|x_{t}-x_{t-1}\|^{2},

(204)

where
(a)
follows from the QG condition implied by the
\mu_{h}
-PŁ
condition of
h
and the
\mu_{J}
-PŁ condition of
J
;
(b)
follows by setting
\mu_{\theta}\triangleq\min\{\mu_{h},\mu_{J}\}>0
;

(c)
applies the recursion in (
196
);

(d)
relies on the
L_{h,1}
-smoothness of
h
, the
L_{J,1}
-smoothness of

J
, and the definition

L_{\theta}\triangleq\max\{L_{h,1},L_{J,1}\}>0
;

(e)
is obtained by applying
\dist^{2}(A,B)\leq 2\,\dist^{2}(A,C)+2\,\dist^{2}(B,C)
to separate the terms involving
x_{t}
and
x_{t-1}
;
and
(f)
is a consequence of Lemma 4.1 of
8
and
Lemma
C.10
, which give the
Lipschitz continuity of
\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x)
and
\theta^{*}(x,\theta)
with
respect to
x
. Note that the distance in
Definition
1
is no greater than the Hausdorff
distance used in
8
.

By choosing
K\geq\frac{1}{\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}}\log\frac{8L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}
, we ensure that
\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\,\dist^{2}(\theta_{t}^{K},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t}))+c\dist^{2}(\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{K},\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{t}}\left(\dist^{2}\left(\theta_{0}^{K},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{0})\right)+c\dist^{2}\left(\tilde{\theta}_{0}^{K},\theta^{*}(x_{0},\theta_{0}^{K})\right)\right)+\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\left(\frac{L_{\theta}^{2}}{\mu^{2}_{\theta}}+C_{\pi}^{2}\right)\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{\|x_{i+1}-x_{i}\|^{2}}{2^{t-i-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{24}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{1}{2^{t-i-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{t}}\frac{2L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}(1-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta})^{K}\left(\dist^{2}\left(\theta_{0}^{0},\theta_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{0})\right)+c\dist^{2}\left(\tilde{\theta}_{0}^{0},\theta^{*}(x_{0},\theta_{0}^{0})\right)\right)+\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{\|x_{i+1}-x_{i}\|^{2}}{2^{t-i-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{48}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}+\frac{1}{2^{t}}\frac{24}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{t}}\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\Delta_{\theta}+\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\frac{\|x_{i+1}-x_{i}\|^{2}}{2^{t-i-1}}+\frac{72}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta},

(205)

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{\theta}\triangleq\dist^{2}\left((\phi_{0},\psi_{0}),\left(\phi_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{0}),\psi^{*}_{\lambda}(x_{0})\right)\right)+c\dist^{2}\left((\phi_{0},\psi_{0}),\left(\phi^{*}(x_{0},\psi_{0}),\psi^{*}(x_{0},\phi_{0})\right)\right)

(206)

is the initial optimality gap of the parameters, and the second inequality is obtained by applying (
200
) at
t=0
..

Substituting this bound back into (
167
), we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2L_{f,1}^{2}+32\lambda^{2}L_{J,1}^{2}+32\lambda^{2}L_{J,1}^{2}C_{\pi}^{2}}{c}\mathbb{E}\left[\dist^{2}(\theta^{*}_{\lambda}(x_{t}),\theta_{t}^{K})+c\dist^{2}(\theta^{*}(x_{t},\theta_{t}^{K}),\tilde{\theta}_{t}^{K})\right]+16\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\frac{1}{2^{t}}\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\Delta_{\theta}+C_{1}\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\|x_{i+1}-x_{i}\|^{2}}{2^{t-i-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{1}\frac{72}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}+16\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2},

(207)

where
L_{\widetilde{g},1}=2L_{J,1}
by Lemma
C.11
and
C_{1}\triangleq\frac{2L_{f,1}^{2}+32\lambda^{2}L_{J,1}^{2}+32\lambda^{2}L_{J,1}^{2}C_{\pi}^{2}}{c}=\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2})
.

Summing the above inequality over
t=0,1,\ldots,T-1
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\Delta_{\theta}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\frac{1}{2^{t}}+C_{1}\frac{4L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\|x_{i+1}-x_{i}\|^{2}}{2^{t-i-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{1}\frac{72}{\mu_{\theta}^{2}\eta_{\theta}}E_{\theta}+16\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\sigma_{H}^{2}

(208)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{1}}{T}\frac{8L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\Delta_{\theta}+C_{1}\frac{8L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\lambda^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}\right)

(209)

where the second inequality follows from the definition of
E_{\theta}
in (
195
) and the geometric-series bounds

\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}2^{-t}\leq 2
and

\sum_{t=i+1}^{T-1}2^{-(t-i-1)}\leq 2
.
∎

Lemma C.19
.

Under Assumptions
1
,

2
, and

3
, suppose that
Algorithm
1
is run with

\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\ell_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-2}).

Proof.

By adding and subtracting
\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]
and
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\ell_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|\leq\left\|\ell_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]\right\|+\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|+\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})-\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|,

(210)

which implies that

\displaystyle\quad\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\ell_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\ell_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]\right\|^{2}\right]+3\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+3\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})-\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\ell_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]\right\|^{2}\right]+3\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-2}),

(211)

where the last inequality follows from Lemma 4.3 in
8
.

Moreover, by Lemma
C.17
, the first error term can be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\ell_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{2\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}+\frac{8\lambda^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}.

(212)

Therefore, telescoping from
t=0
to
T-1
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\ell_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\ell_{t}-\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{3}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}+\frac{24\lambda^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}}{B_{J}}+\frac{3}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-2}).

(213)

∎

Theorem C.1

(Theorem
1
)
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
,

2
, and

3
hold.
Let
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, and let the step sizes satisfy

\displaystyle\eta_{x}\leq\frac{1}{2L_{F}},\quad\eta_{\theta}\asymp\kappa^{-5},\quad\eta_{\phi},\eta_{\psi}\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{L_{J,1}},\frac{1}{\mu_{g}+1},1\right\}.

Then, for the iterates generated by
Algorithm
1
, choosing

K=\mathcal{O}(\log\lambda)
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{F}}{T}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}H^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{B_{J}}\right),

where
\Delta_{F}=\mathbb{E}\left[F(x_{0})-F(x_{T})\right]
.

Choosing
T=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1})
,
\lambda=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1/2})
,
K=\mathcal{O}(\log\epsilon^{-1})
,
H=\mathcal{O}(\log\epsilon^{-1})
,
B_{J}=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2})
and
B=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1})
,
we can obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(\epsilon).

Proof.

By the
L_{F}
-smoothness of
F(x)
in Lemma
C.14
, we have for
\eta_{x}\leq\frac{1}{2L_{F}}
,

\displaystyle F(x_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq F(x_{t})+\langle\nabla F(x_{t}),x_{t+1}-x_{t}\rangle+\frac{L_{F}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=F(x_{t})-\frac{\eta_{x}}{2}\|\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}-(\frac{\eta_{x}}{2}-\frac{\eta_{x}^{2}L_{F}}{2})\|\ell_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{x}}{2}\|\ell_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq F(x_{t})-\frac{\eta_{x}}{2}\|\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}-\frac{1}{4\eta_{x}}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{x}}{2}\|\ell_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}.

(214)

Taking expectation over all the randomness and telescoping
the preceding inequality from
t=0
to
T-1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\mathbb{E}\left[F(x_{0})-F(x_{T})\right]}{\eta_{x}T}+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\ell_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{2\eta_{x}^{2}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}\right].

(215)

By Lemma
C.18
and Lemma
C.19
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\mathbb{E}\left[F(x_{0})-F(x_{T})\right]}{\eta_{x}T}+\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\ell_{t}-\nabla F(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{2\eta_{x}^{2}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}\right]

(216)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\mathbb{E}\left[F(x_{0})-F(x_{T})\right]}{\eta_{x}T}+\frac{6}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbb{E}[\ell_{t}]-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{*}(x_{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-2})

\displaystyle\quad\,-\frac{1}{2\eta_{x}^{2}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}\right]

(217)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\mathbb{E}\left[F(x_{0})-F(x_{T})\right]}{\eta_{x}T}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{-2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}H^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{B_{J}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}e^{-K}\Delta_{\theta}}{T}\right)+\left(48C_{1}\frac{L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)-\frac{1}{2\eta_{x}^{2}}\right)\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}\right],

(218)

where
C_{1}=\frac{2L_{f,1}^{2}+32\lambda^{2}L_{J,1}^{2}+32\lambda^{2}L_{J,1}^{2}C_{\pi}^{2}}{c}=\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2})
and
\Delta_{\theta}
is the initial optimality gap of the parameters defined in (
206
).

By taking
K=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\lambda\right)
such that

\displaystyle 48C_{1}\frac{L_{\theta}}{\mu_{\theta}}\exp(-\mu_{h}\eta_{\theta}K)-\frac{1}{2\eta_{x}^{2}}\leq 0,\quad\mathcal{O}(\frac{\lambda^{2}e^{-K}\Delta_{\theta}}{T})\leq\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{-2}),

we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{F}}{T}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_{f}^{2}}{B}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{2}\gamma^{2H}H^{2})+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{-2}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{B_{J}}\right),

(219)

where
\Delta_{F}=\mathbb{E}\left[F(x_{0})-F(x_{T})\right]
.

Now we consider the requirements for the step sizes to ensure the above bound. For the step size
\eta_{x}
in the outer loop, we just need
\eta_{x}\leq\frac{1}{2L_{F}}
, which is required by the descent lemma of
F(x)
.
For the step sizes
\eta_{\theta},\eta_{\phi},\eta_{\psi}
in the inner loop, we need the conditions in (
189
) to hold. Thus,
\eta_{\phi}
and
\eta_{\psi}
should satisfy
\eta_{\phi},\eta_{\psi}\leq\min\{\frac{1}{L_{J,1}},\frac{1}{\mu_{g}+1},1\}
. Note that
\mu_{J}=\mu_{g}
from Lemma
C.7
and
C.8
. In (
189
), it can also be verified that
c\asymp\kappa^{-2}
and
\kappa_{b}\asymp\kappa^{2}
. Thus,
\frac{\min\{\eta_{\phi},\eta_{\psi}\}\mu_{J}}{2+2\mu_{h}+\frac{\kappa_{b}}{c}}\asymp\kappa^{-5}
, so we can choose
\eta_{\theta}\asymp\kappa^{-5}
.

Choosing
T=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1})
,

\lambda=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1/2})
,

K=\mathcal{O}(\log\epsilon^{-1})
,

H=\mathcal{O}(\log\epsilon^{-1})
,

B_{J}=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2})
, and

B=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\nabla F(x_{t})\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(\epsilon).

(220)

The resulting total sample complexity is

\displaystyle T\cdot K\cdot(B+B_{J})=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\epsilon^{-3}).

∎

Appendix D
Additional Experiment Details

D.1
Synthetic Problem

In this experiment, all algorithms are evaluated using the same environment and initial parameters. The discount factor
\gamma
is set to
0.99
, and the maximum trajectory length is
3
. All algorithms use Monte Carlo sampling to estimate policy gradients, with a batch size of
16
. The regularization coefficients
\tau_{\phi}
and
\tau_{\psi}
are set to
0.1
for all methods. Each algorithm adopts a double-loop structure, with
10
iterations in the inner loop.
For the PANDA algorithm, the learning rate for the UL parameter
x
is set to
0.05
, while the learning rates for the policy parameters
\phi
,
\psi
,
\tilde{\phi}
, and
\tilde{\psi}
are all set to
0.1
. The penalty parameter
\lambda
is set to
4.0
. For the PBRL algorithm, the learning rate for
x
is
0.05
, the learning rates for
\phi
and
\psi
are
0.1
, and we also use a learning rate of
0.1
for updating the policy parameters in the inner loop to estimate
\phi^{*}
and
\psi^{*}
. The penalty parameter
\lambda
is set to
4.0
. For the DA algorithm, the learning rate for
x
is
0.05
, and the learning rates for
\phi
and
\psi
are both
0.1
.

D.2
Sentinel-Intruder

D.2.1
5\times 5
Grid

In this experiment, the environment discount factor
\gamma
is set to
0.99
, the maximum trajectory length is
20
, and the regularization coefficient is set to
0.1
for all algorithms.
The state in the environment is represented as a
4\times 5\times 5
tensor. The first, second, and third channels are one-hot encodings of the sentinel’s position, the intruder’s position, and the target location, respectively, while the fourth channel represents the positions of restricted areas.
The action space of each policy consists of five actions: moving up, down, left, or right, and staying in place.

Each policy is composed of a convolutional layer followed by a fully connected layer. The convolutional layer uses a kernel size of
3
, stride
1
, padding
1
, and outputs
32
channels. After a ReLU activation and a global average pooling operation, the features are passed through a
32\times 5
fully connected layer and a softmax layer to produce a probability distribution over the actions.
The parameterized reward function
r_{x}
has a similar architecture, consisting of a convolutional layer and a fully connected layer. The convolutional layer shares the same structure as that of the policy network and outputs
32
channels. After ReLU activation and global average pooling, a hidden representation of the state is obtained. Meanwhile, the actions of the two policies are mapped to two
8
-dimensional vectors through a fixed embedding layer. The state representation and the two action embeddings are then concatenated and passed through a
48\times 1
fully connected layer and a sigmoid layer to produce the final reward value.

All algorithms use Monte Carlo sampling to estimate policy gradients, with a batch size of
64
. Each algorithm adopts a double-loop structure, with
10
iterations in the inner loop. The learning rates for the UL parameter
x
and the policy parameters
\phi
and
\psi
are selected by grid search over in
\{1,2,5\}\times\{10^{-3},10^{-4},10^{-5}\}
. The penalty parameter
\lambda
is set to
4.0
for both the PANDA and PBRL algorithms.

D.2.2
20\times 20
Grid

Figure 5
:
The
20\times 20
grid environment for the Sentinel-Intruder game. Dark blue cells represent possible sentinel spawn locations, red cells represent possible intruder spawn locations, yellow cells denote restricted areas, and light blue cells indicate target locations.

Figure 6
:
Sentinel-Intruder results on the
5\times 5
grid, averaged over three random seeds. Left: UL loss vs. number of sampled trajectories. Right: LL NE gap vs. number of sampled trajectories.

Figure 7
:
Sentinel-Intruder results on
20\times 20
grid, averaged over three random seeds. Left: UL loss vs. number of sampled trajectories. Right: LL NE gap vs. number of sampled trajectories.

The basic experimental configuration is the same as in the
5\times 5
grid
environment, except for the state dimensionality and network architecture.
In the environment, each state is represented as a
4\times 20\times 20
tensor, where each channel has the same interpretation as in the
5\times 5
grid environment.

We use CNN-based policy networks.
The network contains two convolutional layers with
32
and
64
output channels, respectively.
Both layers use kernel size
3
, stride
1
, and padding
1
, and each is followed by a ReLU activation.
The resulting feature map is flattened and fed into a fully connected layer with
256
hidden units, followed by another ReLU activation.
The final linear layer outputs logits over the
5
discrete actions, and a softmax layer converts these logits into an action probability distribution.
The parameterized reward function
r_{x}
is also implemented by a CNN-based neural reward model.
The state tensor is first encoded by three convolutional layers with
32
,
64
, and
64
output channels, respectively, each using kernel size
3
, stride
1
, and padding
1
, followed by a ReLU activation.
The resulting feature map is processed by adaptive average pooling to obtain a
64
-dimensional state representation.
The actions of the two agents are embedded into two
16
-dimensional vectors, which are concatenated with the state representation.
The concatenated
96
-dimensional feature vector is then passed through an MLP with one hidden layer of dimension
128
, followed by a sigmoid output layer to produce the final reward value.

All algorithms use Monte Carlo sampling to estimate policy gradients, with a batch size of
64
. Each algorithm adopts a double-loop structure, with
10
iterations in the inner loop. The learning rates for the UL parameter
x
and the policy parameters
\phi
and
\psi
are selected from the best combination in
\{1,2,3,5,8\}\times\{10^{-3},10^{-4},10^{-5}\}
. The penalty parameter
\lambda
is set to
4.0
for both the PANDA and PBRL algorithms.

D.2.3
Additional Experimental Results

The results in Figures
6

and
7
show that, in both the
5\times 5
and

20\times 20
grid environments, all algorithms reduce the LL NE gap to
nearly zero, indicating that they can effectively solve the LL problem.
However, PANDA achieves lower UL loss than the baseline algorithms.
